))

\mathbf{D}_{b}\equiv\mathbf{D}_{b}(\mathtt{c}):=\left\{z=E+\mathrm{i}\eta\in\mathbb{C}_{+}:L_{+}-\mathtt{c}\leq E\leq L_{+}+\mathtt{c},\ n^{-1/2-\epsilon_{d}}\leq\eta\leq n^{-1/(d+1)+\epsilon_{d}}\right\}.

(A.2)

Throughout the paper, we will frequently use the minors of a matrix. For the data matrix
Y
in (
2.1
), denote the index set
{\cal I}=\{1,\dots,n\}
. Given an index set
\mathcal{T}\subset{\cal I}
, we introduce
the notation

to denote the

obtained from removing all the

th columns of

for

and keep the original indices of
Y
. In particular,

. For convenience, we briefly write
(\{i\})
,

and

as
(i)
,
(i,j)

and
(ij\mathcal{T})
respectively. Correspondingly, we denote their sample covariance matrices and resolvents as

Q^{(\mathcal{T})}=(Y^{(\mathcal{T})})(Y^{(\mathcal{T})})^{*},\ {\cal Q}^{(\mathcal{T})}=(Y^{(\mathcal{T})})^{*}(Y^{(\mathcal{T})}).

(A.3)

and

G^{(\mathcal{T})}(z)=(Q^{(\mathcal{T})}-zI)^{-1},\quad{\cal G}^{(\mathcal{T})}(z)=({\cal Q}^{(\mathcal{T})}-zI)^{-1}.

(A.4)

Similar to (
2.14
) and Definition
2.7
, we can define
m_{Q}^{(\mathcal{T})}(z)
,
m_{\mathcal{Q}}^{(\mathcal{T})}(z),

m_{1n}^{(\mathcal{T})}(z)
,

and
m_{n}^{(\mathcal{T})}(z)
by removing
\mathbf{y}_{i},i\in\mathcal{T}
or
\xi_{i}^{2},i\in\mathcal{T}.

We begin with the summary of the results when
\xi^{2}
has unbounded support as in Case (i) of Assumption
2.4
. The proofs will be deferred to Section
D.1
. Conditional on some probability event, we provide some useful deterministic estimates for
m_{1n},m_{2n}
and
m_{n}(z)
on the above concerned spectral domain (
A.1
). Denote the control parameter

as follows

e:=\begin{cases}\frac{\log n}{n^{1/\alpha}},&\ \text{if (\ref {ass3.1}) holds};\\
\frac{1}{\log^{1/\beta}n},&\ \text{if (\ref {ass3.2}) holds}.\end{cases}

(A.5)

Lemma A.1
.

Suppose Assumptions
2.1
,
2.6
and (i) of Assumption
2.4
hold. For any fixed realization
\{\xi_{i}^{2}\}\in\Omega
where
\Omega\equiv\Omega_{n}
is some probability event that
\mathbb{P}(\Omega)=1-\mathrm{o}(1)
, we have

1.

For
z\in\mathbf{D}_{u},
we have that for some constants
C_{1},C_{2}>0

\operatorname{Re}m_{1n}(z)\asymp-E^{-1},\ C_{1}\eta E^{-2}\leqslant\operatorname{Im}m_{1n}(z)\leqslant C_{2}\eta E^{-1}.

2.

When
|E-\mu_{1}|\leqslant Cd_{1}
for some sufficiently large constant
C>0,
let
m_{1n}(E)=\lim_{\eta\downarrow 0}m_{1n}(E+\mathrm{i}\eta),
then we have that

m_{1n}(E)\asymp-E^{-1}.

3.

For
z\in\mathbf{D}_{u}
and
e
defined in (
A.5
), we have that

\displaystyle|m_{2n}(z)|=\mathrm{O}(e),\quad|m_{n}(z)|=\mathrm{O}(E^{-1}),

\displaystyle\operatorname{Im}m_{2n}(z)=\mathrm{O}(\eta E^{-1}),\quad\operatorname{Im}m_{n}(z)=\mathrm{O}(\eta E^{-2}).

Remark A.2
.

The above lemma provides some controls for the Stieltjes transforms. Three remarks are in order. First, the construction of the probability event
\Omega
will be given in Section
A.2
. Second, by a discussion similar to (

), conditional on
\Omega
, we can replace
\mu_{1}
with
\varphi\xi^{2}_{(1)}.
Third, The above results hold when we replace

and
m_{n}
with
m_{1n}^{(\mathcal{T})},m_{2n}^{\mathcal{(T)}}
and
m_{n}^{\mathcal{(T)}}
for any finite
\mathcal{T}.

Then we state the results when

has bounded support as in Case (ii) of Assumption

. As mentioned in Remark

we denote the rightmost edge of

as

Moreover, parallel to (

\displaystyle\widehat{\varsigma}_{1}:=\begin{cases}\frac{1}{p}\sum_{j=1}^{n}\frac{l^{2}\xi^{4}_{j}}{(l-\xi^{2}_{j})^{2}},\\
\frac{1}{n}\sum_{j=1}^{n}\frac{l^{2}\xi^{4}_{j}}{(l-\xi^{2}_{j})^{2}}\end{cases}\quad\widehat{\varsigma}_{2}:=\begin{cases}\frac{1}{p}\sum_{j=1}^{n}\frac{l\xi^{2}_{j}}{l-\xi^{2}_{j}},&\text{if (1) of Assumption \ref {assum_model} holds}\\
\frac{1}{n}\sum_{j=1}^{n}\frac{l\xi^{2}_{j}}{l-\xi^{2}_{j}},&\text{if (2) of Assumption \ref {assum_model} holds}\end{cases},

(A.6)

\displaystyle\widehat{\varsigma}_{3}:=\begin{cases}\frac{\phi^{-1}}{p}\sum_{i=1}^{p}\frac{\sigma_{i}^{2}\hat{\varsigma}_{1}}{(\widehat{L}_{+}-\sigma_{i}\hat{\varsigma}_{2})^{2}}\\
\frac{1}{p}\sum_{i=1}^{p}\frac{\sigma_{i}^{2}\hat{\varsigma}_{1}}{(\widehat{L}_{+}-\sigma_{i}\hat{\varsigma}_{2})^{2}},\end{cases}\ \widehat{\varsigma}_{4}:=\begin{cases}\frac{1}{p}\sum_{i=1}^{p}\frac{\sigma_{i}}{(-\widehat{L}_{+}+\sigma_{i}\widehat{\varsigma}_{2})^{2}},&\text{if (1) of Assumption \ref {assum_model} holds}\\
\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{p}\frac{\sigma_{i}}{(-\widehat{L}_{+}+\sigma_{i}\widehat{\varsigma}_{2})^{2}},&\text{if (2) of Assumption \ref {assum_model} holds}\end{cases}.

Furthermore, we need the following spectral parameter set

\mathbf{D}_{b}^{\prime}=\Big\{z\in\mathbf{D}_{b}:|1+\xi^{2}_{j}m_{1n,c}(z)|>\frac{1}{2}n^{-1/(d+1)-\epsilon_{d}},\quad\text{for all}\ 2\leqslant j\leqslant n\Big\}.

(A.7)

Remark A.3
.

We will see from (

Remark A.5
.

Using discussions similar to the paragraphs around equation (5.1) of

Then we provide the results of the averaged local laws.
Throughout the paper, we will consistently use the notion of
stochastic domination
to systematize the statements of the form “
\xi
is bounded by
\zeta
with high probability up to a small power of
n
.”

Definition A.6

(Stochastic domination)
.

(i) Let

\xi=\left(\xi^{(n)}(u):n\in\mathbb{N},u\in U^{(n)}\right),\quad\zeta=\left(\zeta^{(n)}(u):n\in\mathbb{N},u\in U^{(n)}\right),

be two families of nonnegative random variables, where
U^{(n)}
is a possibly
n
-dependent parameter set. We say
\xi
is stochastically dominated by
\zeta
, uniformly in
u
, if for any fixed (small)
\epsilon>0
and (large)
D>0
,

\sup_{u\in U^{(n)}}\mathbb{P}\left(\xi^{(n)}(u)>n^{\epsilon}\zeta^{(n)}(u)\right)\leq n^{-D}

for large enough
n\geq n_{0}(\epsilon,D)
, and we shall use the notation
\xi\prec\zeta
. Throughout this paper, the stochastic domination will always be uniform in all parameters that are not explicitly fixed, such as the matrix indices and the spectral parameter
z
. If for some complex family
\xi
we have
|\xi|\prec\zeta
, then we will also write
\xi\prec\zeta
or
\xi=\mathrm{O}_{\prec}(\zeta)
.

(ii) We say an event
\Xi
holds with high probability if for any constant
D>0
,
\mathbb{P}(\Xi)\geq 1-n^{-D}
for large enough
n
.

Similar to
[
19
,
28
,
79
,
92
]
, instead of working directly with
m_{Q}
and
m_{\mathcal{Q}}
in (
2.14
), it is more convenient to study the following quantities

m_{1}(z)=\begin{cases}\frac{1}{p}{\rm tr}\left(G(z)\Sigma\right),\\
\frac{1}{n}{\rm tr}\left(G(z)\Sigma\right),\\
\end{cases}\quad m_{2}(z)=\begin{cases}\frac{1}{p}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\xi^{2}_{i}\mathcal{G}_{ii}(z),&\ \text{if {Case (1)} of Assumption \ref {assum_model} holds},\\
\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\xi^{2}_{i}\mathcal{G}_{ii}(z)&\ \text{if {Case (2)} of Assumption \ref {assum_model} holds},\\
\end{cases}

(A.20)

Analogously, using the minors in (
A.3
), we can define
m_{1}^{(\mathcal{T})}(z)
and
m_{2}^{(\mathcal{T})}(z).
The following Theorem
A.7
summarizes the averaged local laws for unbounded
\xi^{2}
which will be used in our proof for the main results. Its proof can be found in Section
B.1
.

Theorem A.7

(Averaged local laws for unbounded support
\xi^{2}
)
.

Suppose Assumptions
2.1
,
2.6
and (i) of Assumption
2.4
hold. For any fixed realization
\{\xi_{i}^{2}\}\in\Omega
where
\Omega\equiv\Omega_{n}
is introduced in Lemma
A.1
, let
m_{1}^{(1)}(z)
and
m_{1n}^{(1)}(z)
be defined by removing the column or entries associated with
\xi_{(1)}^{2}.
We have that the followings hold true uniformly for
z\in\mathbf{D}_{u}
in (
A.1
)

1.

If
Case (a)
of (i) of Assumption
2.4
holds, we have that

m_{1}^{(1)}(z)=m_{1n}^{(1)}(z)+\mathrm{O}_{\prec}\left(n^{-1/2-2/\alpha}\right).

2.

If
Case (b)
of (i) of Assumption
2.4
holds, we have that

m_{1}^{(1)}(z)=m_{1n}^{(1)}(z)+\mathrm{O}_{\prec}\left(n^{-1/2}\right).

Then we provide the averaged local laws for bounded
\xi^{2}.
Recall
m_{Q}
defined in (
2.14
).

Theorem A.8

(Averaged local laws for bounded support
\xi^{2}
)
.

Suppose Assumptions
2.1
,
2.6
,
2.10
and (ii) of Assumption
2.4
hold. When
d>1
and
\phi^{-1}>\varsigma_{3},
for any fixed realization
\{\xi_{i}^{2}\}\in\Omega
where
\Omega\equiv\Omega_{n}
is introduced in Lemma
A.4
, for
\eta_{0}
defined in (
3.12
), we have that the followings hold true uniformly for
z\in\mathbf{D}_{b}^{\prime}
in (
A.7
)

\left|m_{1n}(z)-m_{1n,c}(z)\right|\leqslant n^{-1/2+\epsilon_{d}},\ \left|m_{1}(z)-m_{1n}(z)\right|=\mathrm{O}_{\prec}\left((n\eta_{0})^{-1}\right),

and

\left|m_{n}(z)-m_{n,c}(z)\right|\leqslant n^{-1/2+\epsilon_{d}},\ \left|m_{Q}(z)-m_{n}(z)\right|=\mathrm{O}_{\prec}\left((n\eta_{0})^{-1}\right).

A.2
Characterization of ”good configurations”

In this subsection, independent of Section
A.1
, we define some probability events which are some ”good configurations” for the first few largest eigenvalues of
D^{2}.
Our proofs will be restricted on these probability events. In fact, as will be seen in Lemma
A.11
, under Assumption
2.4
, these probability events hold with high probability when
n
is sufficiently large.

Recall Assumption
2.4
and

D^{2}=\operatorname{diag}\left\{\xi_{1}^{2},\cdots,\xi_{n}^{2}\right\}.

Moreover, we define the order statistics of
\{\xi^{2}_{i}\}
as

\xi_{(1)}^{2}\geqslant\xi_{(2)}^{2}\geqslant\cdots\geqslant\xi_{(n)}^{2}.

In what follows, we define these probability events according to the various assumptions of
\{\xi_{i}^{2}\}
in (
2.5
)–(
2.7
).

Definition A.9
.

Denote
\Omega\equiv\Omega_{n}
be the event on
\{\xi_{i}^{2}\}
so that the following conditions hold:

(a). Unbounded support with polynomial decay.
When
\{\xi^{2}_{i}\}
has unbounded support with polynomial decay tail as in (
2.5
), we assume that for all
\epsilon\in(0,1/\alpha)
,
b\in(1/2,1]
and some constants
C,c>1,
the following holds on
\Omega

\displaystyle\xi^{2}_{(1)}-\xi^{2}_{(2)}\geq C^{-1}n^{1/\alpha}\log^{-1}n,

(A.21)

\displaystyle C^{-1}n^{1/\alpha}\log^{-1}n\leq\xi^{2}_{(1)}\leq Cn^{1/\alpha}\log n,

\displaystyle\xi^{2}_{(i)}-\xi^{2}_{(i+1)}\geq C^{-1}n^{\epsilon}\log^{-1}n,\ 1\leqslant i<\sqrt{n},

\displaystyle\xi^{2}_{(1)}-\xi^{2}_{(\lceil n^{b}\rceil)}\geq c^{-1}n^{1/\alpha}\log^{-1}n,

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\xi^{2}_{i}<\infty.

(b). Unbounded support with exponential decay.
When
\{\xi^{2}_{i}\}
has unbounded support with polynomial decay tail as in (
2.6
), we assume that for some constant
C>1,

the following holds on
\Omega

\displaystyle\xi^{2}_{(1)}-\xi^{2}_{(2)}\geq C^{-1}\log^{1/\beta}n,

(A.22)

\displaystyle C^{-1}\log^{1/\beta}n\leq\xi^{2}_{(1)}\leq C\log^{1/\beta}n,

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\xi^{2}_{i}<\infty.

(c). Bounded support with
d>1.
When
\{\xi^{2}_{i}\}
has bounded support satisfying (
2.7
) with
d>1,
we assume that for some sufficiently small constant
\epsilon>0,

\epsilon_{d}<1/8(1/2-1/(d+1))
,
0<b\leqslant 1
and
0<C_{l}<l,
the following holds on
\Omega

\displaystyle n^{-1/(d+1)-\epsilon_{d}}<l-\xi^{2}_{(1)}<n^{-1/(d+1)}\log n,

(A.23)

\displaystyle\xi^{2}_{(1)}-\xi^{2}_{(2)}>n^{-1/(d+1)-\epsilon_{d}},

\displaystyle l-\xi^{2}_{(\lfloor bn\rfloor)}>C_{l},

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\xi^{2}_{i}\leq l,

\displaystyle\left|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\frac{\xi^{2}_{i}}{1+\xi^{2}_{i}m_{1n,c}(z)}-\int\frac{t}{1+tm_{1n,c}(z)}\mathrm{d}F(t)\right|\leq\frac{Cn^{\epsilon}}{\sqrt{n}},\ \text{for}\ z\in\mathbf{D}_{b},

where we recall that
F(t)
is the distribution of
\xi^{2}
and
C>0
is some generic constant.

Remark A.10
.

Two remarks are in order. First, on the event
\Omega,
for the unbounded support case, according to (a) and (b), we see that the first few largest
\xi_{i}^{2}
are divergent and well separated from each other. Second, for the bounded support case, we only provide the results for
d>1
in (c). Nevertheless, it is easy to see that similar results can be obtained for
-1<d\leqslant 1.

The following lemma shows that under Assumption
2.4
, the probability event
\Omega
happens with high probability in all the four settings. The proof will be given in Section
D.2
.

Lemma A.11
.

Let
\Omega
be the events defined in Definition
A.9
, suppose Assumption
2.4
holds, we then have that when
n
is sufficiently large

\mathbb{P}(\Omega)=1-\mathrm{O}(\log^{-D}n),

for some constant
D>0
.

A.3
Some useful lemmas and a summary of extreme value theory

In this subsection, we first provide some technical lemmas which will be used in our proof. The following resolvent identities play an important role in our proof. Recall the resolvents defined in (
2.13
) and the minors defined in (
A.3
).

Lemma A.12

(Resolvent identities)
.

Let
\{\mathbf{y}_{i}\}\subset\mathbb{R}^{p}
be the columns of
Y
as in (
2.1
), then we have that

\displaystyle\mathcal{G}_{ii}(z)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{z+z\mathbf{y}_{i}^{*}{G}^{(i)}(z)\mathbf{y}_{i}},

\displaystyle\mathcal{G}_{ij}(z)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle z\mathcal{G}_{ii}(z)\mathcal{G}_{jj}^{(i)}(z)\mathbf{y}_{i}^{*}{G}^{(ij)}(z)\mathbf{y}_{j}\quad i\neq j,

\displaystyle\mathcal{G}_{ij}(z)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathcal{G}_{ij}^{(k)}(z)+\frac{\mathcal{G}_{ik}(z)\mathcal{G}_{kj}(z)}{\mathcal{G}_{kk}(z)}\quad i,j\neq k.

Proof.

The proof is straightforward using Schur’s complement formula; for example see
[
80
, Lemma 2.3]
.
∎

Lemma A.13

(Some useful matrix identities)
.

For any finite subset
\mathcal{T}\subset\{1,\dots,n\}
, we have that

\left\|G^{(\mathcal{T})}\Sigma^{1/2}\right\|^{2}_{F}=\eta^{-1}\operatorname{Im}\operatorname{Tr}\left(G^{(\mathcal{T})}\Sigma\right).

(A.24)

Moreover, we have that

\displaystyle\left|{\rm Tr}(\mathcal{G}^{(i)}-\mathcal{G})\right|\leq\eta^{-1},

\displaystyle\left|{\rm Tr}({G}^{(i)}-{G})\right|\leq|z|^{-1}+\eta^{-1},

(A.25)

\displaystyle\left|\operatorname{Im}{\rm Tr}({G}^{(i)}-{\cal G})\right|\leq\eta|z|^{-2}+\eta^{-1}.

Proof.

Due to similarity, we focus our discussion on the separable covariance i.i.d. data, i.e., Case (2) of Assumption
2.1
. In fact, it is easier to handle Case (1) since
\Sigma
can be always assumed to be diagonal.

We start with the proof of (
A.24
). Recall (
A.4
). We can write

G^{(\mathcal{T})}=\left(\Sigma^{1/2}X^{(\mathcal{T})}D^{2}X^{(\mathcal{T})}\Sigma^{1/2}-z\right)^{-1}.

Let the spectral decomposition
\Sigma=U\Lambda U^{*}.
Observe that

\displaystyle\|G^{(\mathcal{T})}\Sigma^{1/2}\|_{F}^{2}

\displaystyle=\operatorname{Tr}\left(\left(\Sigma^{1/2}X^{(\mathcal{T})}D^{2}X^{(\mathcal{T})}\Sigma^{1/2}-z\right)^{-1}U\Lambda U^{*}\left(\Sigma^{1/2}X^{(\mathcal{T})}D^{2}X^{(\mathcal{T})}\Sigma^{1/2}-\bar{z}\right)^{-1}\right)

\displaystyle=\operatorname{Tr}\left(U\left(\Lambda^{1/2}U^{*}X^{(\mathcal{T})}D^{2}X^{(\mathcal{T})}U\Lambda^{1/2}-z\right)^{-1}U^{*}U\Lambda U^{*}U\left(\Lambda^{1/2}U^{*}X^{(\mathcal{T})}D^{2}X^{(\mathcal{T})}U\Lambda^{1/2}-\bar{z}\right)^{-1}U^{*}\right)

\displaystyle=\left\|\left(\Lambda^{1/2}U^{*}X^{(\mathcal{T})}D^{2}X^{(\mathcal{T})}U\Lambda^{1/2}-z\right)^{-1}\Lambda^{1/2}\right\|_{F}^{2}

\displaystyle=\eta^{-1}\operatorname{Im}\operatorname{Tr}\left[\left(\Lambda^{1/2}U^{*}X^{(\mathcal{T})}D^{2}X^{(\mathcal{T})}U\Lambda^{1/2}-z\right)^{-1}\Lambda\right],

\displaystyle=\eta^{-1}\operatorname{Im}\operatorname{Tr}\left[U^{*}U\left(\Lambda^{1/2}U^{*}X^{(\mathcal{T})}D^{2}X^{(\mathcal{T})}U\Lambda^{1/2}-z\right)^{-1}U^{*}U\Lambda\right],

\displaystyle=\eta^{-1}\operatorname{Im}\operatorname{Tr}\left(G^{(\mathcal{T})}\Sigma\right),

where in the fourth step we used Ward’s identity (see the equation below (4.42) of
[
23
]
).

Second, the proof of (
A.25
) follows from the definitions of the resolvents; see
[
27
, Lemma A.4]
and the proof of
[
9
, Lemma 4.6]
for more detail.

∎

Lemma A.14

(Large deviation bounds)
.

Let
\mathbf{u}=(u_{1},u_{2},\cdots,u_{p})^{*},\widetilde{\mathbf{u}}=(\widetilde{u}_{1},\widetilde{u}_{2},\cdots,\widetilde{u}_{p})^{*}\in\mathbb{R}^{p}
be two real independent random vectors. Moreover, let
A
be a
p\times p
matrix independent of the above vectors. Then the following holds.

(1).

When the entries of the random vectors are centered i.i.d. random variables with variance
n^{-1}
and
\mathbb{E}|\sqrt{n}v_{i}|^{k}\leqslant C_{k},
where
v_{i}=u_{i},\widetilde{u}_{i},1\leqslant i\leqslant p,
then we have that

\displaystyle|\widetilde{\mathbf{u}}^{*}\mathbf{u}|\prec\sqrt{\frac{\|\mathbf{u}\|^{2}}{n},}\ \ \left|\mathbf{u}^{*}A\tilde{\mathbf{u}}\right|\prec\frac{1}{n}\|A\|_{F},\ \ |\mathbf{u}^{*}A\mathbf{u}-\frac{1}{n}{\rm Tr}A|\prec\frac{1}{n}\|A\|_{F}.

(2).

When the random vectors are sampled from
\mathsf{U}(\mathbb{S}^{p-1}),
then we have that

\displaystyle|\widetilde{\mathbf{u}}^{*}\mathbf{u}|\prec\sqrt{\frac{\|\mathbf{u}\|^{2}}{p},}\ \ \left|\mathbf{u}^{*}A\tilde{\mathbf{u}}\right|\prec\frac{1}{p}\|A\|_{F},\ \ |\mathbf{u}^{*}A\mathbf{u}-\frac{1}{p}{\rm Tr}A|\prec\frac{1}{p}\|A\|_{F}.

Proof.

The proof of (1) can be found in Lemma 3.4 of
[
80
]
or Lemma 5.6 of
[
92
]
; the proof of (2) can be found in Lemma I.3 of
[
90
]
.
∎

In what follows, we provide a mini-review of the extreme value theory for a sequence of i.i.d. random variables following
[
48
]
. For more systematic treatments, we refer to the monographs
[
10
,
18
,
43
,
83
]
.

Lemma A.15

(Fisher-Tippett-Gnedenko Theorem)
.

Let
\{x_{i}^{2}\}
be a sequence of i.i.d. random variables and denote
M_{n}:=x_{(1)}^{2}
as the largest order statistic.

1.

If there exist some constants
\alpha_{n}>0
and
\beta_{n}\in\mathbb{R}
and some non-degenerate
cdf
G such that
\alpha_{n}^{-1}(M_{n}-\beta_{n})
converges in distribution to G, then
G
belongs to the type of one of the following three
cdfs:

\begin{split}&\text{Gumbel}:\ G_{0}(x)=\exp(-e^{-x}),\ x\in\mathbb{R},\\
&\text{Fr{\'{e}}chet}:\ G_{1,\alpha}(x)=\exp(-x^{-\alpha}),\ x\geqslant 0,\ \alpha>0,\\
&\text{Weibull}:\ G_{2,\alpha}(x)=\exp(-|x|^{\alpha}),\ x\leqslant 0,\ \alpha>0.\end{split}

2.

Recall (
3.1
). First, if
\{x_{i}\}
satisfies (
2.5
), we have that

\frac{M_{n}}{b_{n}}\overset{d}{\Rightarrow}G_{1,\alpha},

Moreover, if we futher assume
\lim_{x\uparrow\infty}L(x)=\mathsf{C}
for some constant
\mathsf{C}>0,
then
b_{n}=(\mathsf{C}n)^{1/\alpha}.
Second, if
\{x_{i}\}
satisfies (
2.6
) and (
2.10
), we have that

\mathsf{g}^{\prime}(b_{n})(M_{n}-b_{n})\overset{d}{\Rightarrow}G_{0}.

Finally, if (
2.7
) holds, recall
\mathfrak{b}
in (
2.8
), we have that

(\mathfrak{b}n)^{1/(d+1)}(M_{n}-l)\overset{d}{\Rightarrow}G_{2,d+1}.

Proof.

The proof can be found in the standard textbook or review article regarding extreme value theory. For example, see
[
48
]
and
[
10
]
.
∎

Appendix B
Proof of averaged local laws

In this section, we prove the local laws Theorems
A.7
and
A.8
. Throughout the proof, due to similarity, we focus on the discussion of the separable covariance i.i.d. model as in Case (2) of Assumption
2.1
and only briefly explain the main differences from the elliptically distributed model as in Case (1) of Assumption
2.1
.

B.1
Unbounded support setting: proof of Theorem
A.7

In this section, we will prove Theorem
A.7
. Due to similarity, we focus on the proof of part 1 and briefly discuss that of part 2. The proof contains two steps. In the first step, we will establish the results for the results of
Q
outside the bulk of the spectrum on the domain
\widetilde{\mathbf{D}}_{u}
denoted as follows

\widetilde{\mathbf{D}}_{u}\equiv\widetilde{\mathbf{D}}_{u}(\mathtt{C}):=\left\{z=E+\mathrm{i}\eta:0<E-\mu_{1}\leqslant\mathtt{C}d_{1},\ n^{-2/3}\leq\eta\leq\mathtt{C}\mu_{1}\right\}.

(B.1)

That is, we will establish the following proposition.

Proposition B.1
.

Under the assumptions of Theorem
A.7
, the following results hold uniformly on the spectral domain
\widetilde{\mathbf{D}}_{u}
in (
B.1
) when conditional on the event
\Omega
in Lemma
A.11
.

(1).

If
Case (a)
of (i) of Assumption
2.4
holds, we have that

\displaystyle\mathcal{G}_{ij}(z)=-\frac{\delta_{ij}}{z(1+m_{1n}(z)\xi_{i}^{2})}+\mathrm{O}_{\prec}\left(n^{-1/2-1/\alpha}\right),

(B.2)

where
\delta_{ij}
is the Dirac delta function so that
\delta_{ij}=1
when
i=j
and
\delta_{ij}=0
when
i\neq j.
Moreover, we have that

m_{1}(z)=m_{1n}(z)+\mathrm{O}_{\prec}\left(n^{-1/2-2/\alpha}\right),\ m_{2}(z)=m_{2n}(z)+\mathrm{O}_{\prec}\left(n^{-1/2-1/\alpha}\right),

and

m_{Q}(z)=m_{n}(z)+\mathrm{O}_{\prec}\left(n^{-1/2-2/\alpha}\right).

(B.3)

(2).

If
Case (b)
of (i) of Assumption
2.4
holds, we have that the results in part (1) hold by setting
\alpha=\infty.

Once Proposition
B.1
is proved, we can quantify the rough locations of the eigenvalues of
Q
as summarized in the following lemma.

Lemma B.2
.

Suppose Assumptions
2.1
,
2.6
and (i) of Assumption
2.4
hold. For some sufficiently large constant
C>0,
with high probability, for any fixed realization
\{\xi_{i}^{2}\}\in\Omega
where
\Omega\equiv\Omega_{n}
is introduced in Lemma
A.11
, for all
1\leqslant i\leqslant\min\{p,n\},
we have that

\lambda_{i}(Q)\notin(\mu_{1},Cn^{1/\alpha}\log n),\ \text{if {Case (i)-a} of Assumption \ref {assum_D} holds},

(B.4)

and

\lambda_{i}(Q)\notin(\mu_{1},C\log^{1/\beta}n),\ \text{if {Case (i)-b of Assumption \ref {assum_D} holds}}.

(B.5)

Proof.

Due to similarity, we focus our arguments on (
B.4
). We prove the results by contradiction.
Assume there is an eigenvalue of
Q
lies in the interval as in (
B.4
), denote as
\widehat{\lambda}
. Let
z=\hat{\lambda}+\mathrm{i}n^{-2/3}.
Since
z\in\widetilde{\mathbf{D}}_{u}\subset\mathbf{D}_{u}
as in (
A.1
), by Lemma
A.1
, we obtain
\operatorname{Im}m_{n}(z)=\eta\widehat{\lambda}^{-2}
. According to (
C.7
) and (
A.21
), we have that on the event
\Omega

\mu_{1}\gtrsim n^{1/\alpha}\log^{-1}n.

(B.6)

Together with (
B.3
), we readily see that

\begin{split}\operatorname{Im}m_{Q}(z)&=\operatorname{Im}m_{n}(z)+\operatorname{Im}(m_{Q}(z)-m_{n}(z))\\
&\prec n^{-2/\alpha-2/3}+n^{-1/2-2/\alpha}\prec n^{-1/2-2/\alpha}.\end{split}

(B.7)

On the other hand, we have

\operatorname{Im}m_{Q}(z)=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i}\frac{\eta}{(\lambda_{i}-\widehat{\lambda})^{2}+\eta^{2}}\geq\frac{1}{n\eta}=n^{-1/3},

which contradicts (
B.7
). Therefore, there is no eigenvalue in this interval. Similarly, we can prove (
B.5
). The only difference is that (
B.6
) should be replaced by
\mu_{1}\gtrsim\log^{1/\beta}n
according to (
A.22
) so that the error rate in (
B.7
) should be updated to
n^{-1/2}.
This completes the proof.
∎

Armed with the above lemma, we can proceed to the second step to conclude the proof of Theorem
A.7
. In what follows, we first provide the proof of Proposition
B.1
in Section
B.1.1
. After that, we prove Theorem
A.7
in Section
B.1.2
.

B.1.1
Proof of Proposition
B.1

We first prepare two lemmas. The first one is to establish Proposition
B.1
for large scale of
\eta.

Lemma B.3

(Average local law for large
\eta
)
.

Proposition
B.1
holds when
\eta=\mathtt{C}\mu_{1}.

Proof.

As mentioned before, due to similarity, we focus on the study of the separable covariance i.i.d. model as in Case (2) of Assumption
2.1
when
\xi^{2}
satisfies (
2.5
). The main differences from the other cases will be explained in the end of the proof. In what follows, without loss of generality, we assume that
\xi_{1}^{2}\geqslant\xi_{2}^{2}\geqslant\cdots\geqslant\xi_{n}^{2}.

Note that according to (
C.7
), (
A.21
) and the definition of
d_{1}
in (
3.15
), on the event
\Omega,
we have that

E\asymp\xi_{1}^{2}.

(B.8)

When
\eta=\mathtt{C}E
, we have
\max\left\{\|G^{(\mathcal{T})}\|,\|\mathcal{G}^{(\mathcal{T})}\|\right\}\leq\eta^{-1}=\mathtt{C}^{-1}E^{-1}
for any finite
\mathcal{T}\subset\{1,\dots,n\}
by definition. The main idea is to explore the relation of
m_{1}
and
m_{2}
using Lemma
A.12
. We start with
m_{2}.
By Lemma
A.12
and definition of
m_{2}
in (
A.20
), we have

\displaystyle{m_{2}=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\frac{\xi^{2}_{i}}{-z-z\mathbf{y}^{*}_{i}G^{(i)}\mathbf{y}_{i}}=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\frac{\xi^{2}_{i}}{-z(1+\xi^{2}_{i}n^{-1}{\rm tr}G^{(i)}\Sigma+Z_{i})},}

(B.9)

\displaystyle Z_{i}=\mathbf{y}^{*}_{i}G^{(i)}\mathbf{y}_{i}-\xi^{2}_{i}n^{-1}{\rm tr}G^{(i)}\Sigma.

As
\mathbf{y}_{i}
is independent of
G^{(i)}
, by (1) of Lemma
A.14
, we see that

Z_{i}\prec\frac{\xi^{2}_{i}}{n}\|G^{(i)}\Sigma\|_{F}\leq\frac{\xi^{2}_{i}}{n}\|G^{(i)}\|\|\Sigma\|_{F}\prec\frac{\xi^{2}_{i}}{\sqrt{n}\eta}.

(B.10)

Moreover, using the definition of
m_{1}
in (
A.20
) and the second resolvent identity, we readily obtain that for some constant
C>0

\frac{1}{n}{\rm tr}(G^{(i)}\Sigma)-m_{1}(z)=\frac{1}{n}\mathbf{y}_{i}^{*}G\Sigma G^{(i)}\mathbf{y}_{i}\leq C\frac{\xi^{2}_{i}}{n\eta^{2}}.

(B.11)

Moreover, by (
B.8
) and the form of
\eta,
we find that for some constant
C>0

|1+\xi^{2}_{i}m_{1}|\geq 1-C\mathtt{C}^{-1}>0,

when
\mathtt{C}>0
is chosen to be sufficiently large.
Together with (
B.9
), we obtain

m_{2}=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\frac{\xi^{2}_{i}}{-z(1+\xi^{2}_{i}m_{1}+\mathrm{O}_{\prec}(\frac{\xi^{2}_{i}}{\sqrt{n}\eta}))}=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\frac{\xi^{2}_{i}}{-z(1+\xi^{2}_{i}m_{1})}+\mathrm{O}_{\prec}(n^{-1/2-1/\alpha}),

(B.12)

where we used (
A.21
).

Then we work with
m_{1}.
Decompose that

Q-zI=\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbf{y}_{i}\mathbf{y}_{i}^{*}+zm_{2}(z)\Sigma-z(I+m_{2}(z)\Sigma).

Applying resolvent expansion to the order one, we obtain that

G=-z^{-1}(I+m_{2}(z)\Sigma)^{-1}+z^{-1}G\left(\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbf{y}_{i}\mathbf{y}_{i}^{*}+zm_{2}(z)\Sigma\right)(I+m_{2}(z)\Sigma)^{-1}.

Furthermore, using Shernman-Morrison formula, we have that

G\mathbf{y}_{i}=\frac{G^{(i)}\mathbf{y}_{i}}{1+\mathbf{y}_{i}^{*}G^{(i)}\mathbf{y}_{i}}.

(B.13)

Combining the above two identities and Lemma
A.12
, we can further write

\begin{split}G&=-z^{-1}(I+m_{2}(z)\Sigma)^{-1}+\left[z^{-1}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\frac{G^{(i)}(\mathbf{y}_{i}\mathbf{y}_{i}^{*}-n^{-1}\xi^{2}_{i}\Sigma)}{1+\mathbf{y}_{i}^{*}G^{(i)}\mathbf{y}_{i}}(I+m_{2}(z)\Sigma)^{-1}\right]\\
&+\left[z^{-1}\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\frac{(G^{(i)}-G)\xi^{2}_{i}\Sigma}{1+\mathbf{y}_{i}^{*}G^{(i)}\mathbf{y}_{i}}(I+m_{2}(z)\Sigma)^{-1}\right]\\
&:=-z^{-1}(I+m_{2}(z)\Sigma)^{-1}+R_{1}+R_{2}.\end{split}

(B.14)

In what follows, we control the two error terms
R_{1},R_{2}.
For
R_{1}
, we notice that

\begin{split}&\frac{z}{n}{\rm tr}(R_{1}\Sigma)=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i}{\rm tr}\left(\frac{G^{(i)}(\mathbf{y}_{i}\mathbf{y}_{i}^{*}-n^{-1}\xi^{2}_{i}\Sigma)}{1+\mathbf{y}_{i}^{*}G^{(i)}\mathbf{y}_{i}}(I+m_{2}^{(i)}\Sigma)^{-1}\Sigma\right)\\
&+\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i}{\rm tr}\left(\frac{G^{(i)}(\mathbf{y}_{i}\mathbf{y}_{i}^{*}-n^{-1}\xi^{2}_{i}\Sigma)}{1+\mathbf{y}_{i}^{*}G^{(i)}\mathbf{y}_{i}}(I+m_{2}\Sigma)^{-1}(m_{2}^{(i)}-m_{2})\Sigma(I+m^{(i)}_{2}\Sigma)^{-1}\Sigma\right)\\
&:=\mathtt{R}_{11}+\mathtt{R}_{12}.\end{split}

(B.15)

Since
\|\mathcal{G}^{(i)}\|\leq\eta^{-1}
, using (
B.8
), (
A.21
), with high probability, we have that for some constant
C>0,

|m_{2}^{(i)}(z)|\leq\frac{1}{n}\sum_{j\neq i}\xi^{2}_{j}|\mathcal{G}^{(i)}_{jj}|\leq C\frac{\log^{2}n}{n^{1/\alpha}}.

(B.16)

Moreover, according to (
B.10
), with high probability, when
n
is sufficiently large, we have that for some constant
C>0

|1+\mathbf{y}_{i}^{*}G^{(i)}\mathbf{y}_{i}|\asymp|1+\xi_{i}^{2}n^{-1}{\rm tr}G^{(i)}\Sigma|\geqslant 1-C\mathtt{C}^{-1}>0,

(B.17)

whenever
\mathtt{C}
is chosen sufficiently large. Consequently, for all
i
, we have that

\begin{split}&{\rm tr}\left(\frac{G^{(i)}(\mathbf{y}_{i}\mathbf{y}_{i}^{*}-n^{-1}\xi^{2}_{i}\Sigma)}{1+\mathbf{y}_{i}^{*}G^{(i)}\mathbf{y}_{i}}(I+m_{2}^{(i)}\Sigma)^{-1}\Sigma\right)\asymp{\rm tr}\left(\xi^{2}_{i}G^{(i)}(\mathbf{u}_{i}\mathbf{u}_{i}^{*}-n^{-1}I)(I+m_{2}^{(i)}\Sigma)^{-1}\Sigma^{2}\right)\\
&=\xi_{i}^{2}\left(\mathbf{u}_{i}^{*}G^{(i)}(I+m_{2}^{(i)}\Sigma)^{-1}\Sigma^{2}\mathbf{u}_{i}-n^{-1}{\rm tr}\left(G^{(i)}(I+m_{2}^{(i)}\Sigma)^{-1}\Sigma^{2}\right)\right)\\
&\prec\xi_{i}^{2}\frac{1}{\eta\sqrt{n}},\end{split}

(B.18)

where in the third step we used (1) of Lemma
A.14
. Together with (
A.21
) and (
B.8
), we find that

\mathtt{R}_{11}\prec n^{-1/2-1/\alpha}.

For
\mathtt{R}_{12}
, using the definition in (
A.20
) and the identity in Lemma
A.12
and the definition of
\mathcal{G}^{(i)}
(see (
B.28
) below), we see that

m_{2}(z)-m^{(i)}_{2}(z)=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{j=1}^{n}\xi^{2}_{j}(\mathcal{G}_{jj}-\mathcal{G}^{(i)}_{jj})=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{j\neq i}\xi^{2}_{j}\frac{\mathcal{G}_{ji}\mathcal{G}_{ij}}{\mathcal{G}_{ii}}+\frac{\xi_{i}^{2}(\mathcal{G}_{ii}-|z|^{-1})}{n}.

(B.19)

In addition, using Lemma
A.12
and a discussion similar to (
B.17
), we conclude that

\frac{1}{\mathcal{G}_{ii}(z)}=-z-z\mathbf{y}_{i}^{*}G^{(i)}\mathbf{y}_{i}\prec|z|.

Moreover, by Lemmas
A.12
and
A.14
, we have that

\mathcal{G}_{ij}(z)=z\mathcal{G}_{ii}(z)\mathcal{G}^{(i)}_{jj}(z)\mathbf{y}_{i}^{*}G^{(ij)}\mathbf{y}_{j}\prec|z|\eta^{-2}|\xi_{i}\xi_{j}|n^{-1}\|\mathcal{G}^{(ij)}\|_{F}\prec n^{-1/2}|z|\eta^{-3}|\xi_{i}\xi_{j}|,\quad i\neq j.

Combining the above bounds with (
A.21
), we see that

m_{2}(z)-m^{(i)}_{2}(z)\prec n^{-1-1/\alpha}.

Together with (
B.17
) and (
B.18
), we arrive at

\mathtt{R}_{12}\prec\frac{1}{\eta^{2}n^{3/2}}.

Using the above bounds, we see that

\frac{z}{n}{\rm tr}(R_{1}\Sigma)\prec n^{-1/2-1/\alpha}.

For
R_{2}
, applying the Sherman–Morrison formula to
((G^{(i)})^{-1}+\mathbf{y}_{i}\mathbf{y}_{i}^{*})^{-1},
we obtain that

\displaystyle\begin{split}\frac{1}{n}\left|{\rm tr}\left(\frac{(G^{(i)}-G)\Sigma(I+m_{2}\Sigma)^{-1}\Sigma}{1+\mathbf{y}_{i}^{*}G^{(i)}\mathbf{y}_{i}}\right)\right|&=\frac{1}{n}\left|\frac{\mathbf{y}_{i}^{*}G^{(i)}\Sigma(I+m_{2}\Sigma)^{-1}\Sigma G\mathbf{y}_{i}}{1+\mathbf{y}_{i}^{*}G^{(i)}\mathbf{y}_{i}}\right|\\
&\prec\frac{\xi_{i}^{2}}{n\eta^{2}},\end{split}

(B.20)

where in the second step we used Lemma
A.14
and (
B.17
) and a discussion similar to (
B.16
). Together with the definition of
R_{2}
in (
B.14
), by (
A.21
), we find that

\begin{split}\frac{z}{n}\left|{\rm tr}(R_{2}\Sigma)\right|\leq\frac{1}{n^{2}}\sum_{i}\frac{\xi^{2}_{i}}{\eta^{2}}\prec n^{-1-2/\alpha}.\end{split}

As a result, in light of the definition
m_{1}
in (
A.20
),
we have

\begin{split}m_{1}&=\frac{1}{n}{\rm tr}(G(z)\Sigma)=-z^{-1}\frac{1}{n}{\rm tr}((I+m_{2}(z)\Sigma)^{-1}\Sigma)+\mathrm{O}_{\prec}(n^{-1/2-2/\alpha})\\
&=-\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{p}\frac{\sigma_{i}}{z(1+m_{2}\sigma_{i})}+\mathrm{O}_{\prec}(n^{-1/2-2/\alpha}).\end{split}

(B.21)

We first control
m_{2}(z)-m_{2n}(z).
Recall
m_{2n}(z)
in (
2.16
). Combing (
B.12
) and (
B.21
), we have that

\displaystyle m_{2}(z)-m_{2n}(z)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left(\frac{\xi^{2}_{i}}{-z(1+\xi^{2}_{i}m_{1})}+\frac{\xi^{2}_{i}}{z(1+\xi^{2}_{i}m_{1n})}\right)+\mathrm{O}_{\prec}(n^{-1/2-1/\alpha})

(B.22)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\frac{\xi^{4}_{i}(m_{1}-m_{1n})}{z(1+\xi^{2}_{i}m_{1})(1+\xi^{2}_{i}m_{1n})}+\mathrm{O}_{\prec}(n^{-1/2-1/\alpha})

\displaystyle=\left(\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\frac{\xi^{4}_{i}}{z(1+\xi^{2}_{i}m_{1})(1+\xi^{2}_{i}m_{1n})}\right)\left(\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\frac{\sigma_{i}^{2}(m_{2}-m_{2n})}{z(1+\sigma_{i}m_{2})(1+\sigma_{i}m_{2n})}\right)+\mathrm{O}_{\prec}(n^{-1/2-1/\alpha}).

By a discussion similar to (
B.16
) and (
B.17
) with high probability, when
n
is sufficiently large, we have that

\begin{split}|m_{2}-m_{2n}|&=\mathrm{O}\left(\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\frac{\xi^{4}_{i}}{z^{2}}|m_{2}-m_{2n}|\right)+\mathrm{O}_{\prec}(n^{-1/2-1/\alpha})\\
&=\mathrm{O}(n^{-2/\alpha}|m_{2}-m_{2n}|)+\mathrm{O}_{\prec}(n^{-1/2-1/\alpha}),\end{split}

where in the second step we used (
A.21
). Then we can conclude that
m_{2}-m_{2n}\prec n^{-1/2-1/\alpha}
. By a similar procedure, we also have
m_{1}-m_{1n}\prec n^{-1/2-2/\alpha}
.

Armed with the above two results, we proceed to finish the rest of the proof. Recall
m_{Q}
in (
2.14
). Using (
B.14
) and a discussion similar to (
B.21
), one can see that

\begin{split}m_{\mathcal{Q}}&=\frac{1}{p}{\rm tr}(G(z))=-\frac{1}{p}\sum_{i=1}^{p}\frac{1}{z(1+m_{2}\sigma_{i})}+\mathrm{O}_{\prec}(n^{-1/2-2/\alpha})\\
&=-\frac{1}{p}\sum_{i=1}^{p}\frac{1}{z(1+m_{2n}\sigma_{i})}+\frac{1}{p}\sum_{i=1}^{p}\frac{(m_{2}-m_{2n})\sigma_{i}}{z(1+m_{2}\sigma_{i})(1+m_{2n}\sigma_{i})}+\mathrm{O}_{\prec}(n^{-1/2-2/\alpha})\\
&=m_{n}+\mathrm{O}_{\prec}(n^{-1/2-2/\alpha}),\end{split}

(B.23)

where in the last step we recall
m_{n}(z)
in (
2.16
). Finally, for the control of the matrix
\mathcal{G},
for the diagonal entries, by Lemma
A.12
and a discussion similar to (
B.10
) and
B.11
, we have

\begin{split}\mathcal{G}_{ii}&=-\frac{1}{z(1+\mathbf{y}^{*}_{i}G^{(i)}\mathbf{y}_{i})}=-\frac{1}{z(1+\xi^{2}_{i}n^{-1}{\rm tr}G^{(i)}\Sigma+\mathrm{O}_{\prec}(\frac{\xi^{2}_{i}}{\sqrt{n}\eta}))}\\
&=-\frac{1}{z(1+\xi^{2}_{i}m_{1}+\mathrm{O}_{\prec}(\frac{\xi^{2}_{i}}{\sqrt{n}\eta}))}=-\frac{1}{z(1+\xi^{2}_{i}m_{1n})}+\mathrm{O}_{\prec}(n^{-1/2-1/\alpha}).\end{split}

For off-diagonal entries, together with Lemmas
A.12
and
A.14
, we have

\begin{split}|\mathcal{G}_{ij}|&\leq|z||\mathcal{G}_{ii}||\mathcal{G}^{(i)}_{ii}||\mathbf{y}^{*}_{i}G^{(ij)}\mathbf{y}_{j}|\prec n^{-1/2-2/\alpha}.\end{split}

This completes the proof when (
2.5
) holds. For the case (
2.6
), the main difference is to use the estimates of (
A.22
) instead of (
A.21
) whenever it is needed, for example, (
B.9
). We omit further details.

For the elliptical model as in Case (1) of Assumption
2.1
, the discussions are similar by using the definitions in (
2.15
) and (
A.20
) with (2) of Lemma
A.14
. The main difference is that (
B.14
) should be replaced by

\begin{split}G&=-z^{-1}(I+m_{2}(z)\Sigma)^{-1}+\left[z^{-1}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\frac{G^{(i)}(\mathbf{y}_{i}\mathbf{y}_{i}^{*}-p^{-1}\xi^{2}_{i}\Sigma)}{1+\mathbf{y}_{i}^{*}G^{(i)}\mathbf{y}_{i}}(I+m_{2}(z)\Sigma)^{-1}\right]\\
&+\left[z^{-1}\frac{1}{p}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\frac{(G^{(i)}-G)\xi^{2}_{i}\Sigma}{1+\mathbf{y}_{i}^{*}G^{(i)}\mathbf{y}_{i}}(I+m_{2}(z)\Sigma)^{-1}\right].\end{split}

The rest can then be proved verbatim with some minor changes. We omit further details due to similarity. This completes our proof.

∎

The second component is to prove Proposition
B.1
under a priori control of the resolvent which is summarized in the following lemma.

Lemma B.4
.

Proposition
B.1
holds if (
B.2
) holds uniformly for
z\in\widetilde{\mathbf{D}}_{u}.

Proof.

As before, due to similarity, we focus on the study of the separable covariance i.i.d. model as in Case (2) of Assumption
2.1
when
\xi^{2}
satisfies (
2.5
).

Note that according to the priori control (
B.2
), we have that for
1\leqslant i\neq j\leqslant n

\displaystyle\mathcal{G}_{ii}=\frac{1}{z(1+\xi^{2}_{i}m_{1n}(z)}+\mathrm{O}_{\prec}(n^{-1/2-1/\alpha}),\quad\mathcal{G}_{ij}=\mathrm{O}_{\prec}(n^{-1/2-1/\alpha}).

(B.24)

For the diagonal entries, when
i=1,
using (
3.14
), we observe that

\displaystyle\begin{split}\mathcal{G}_{11}&=-\frac{1}{z(1+\xi^{2}_{1}m_{1n}(z))}+\mathrm{O}_{\prec}(n^{-1/2-1/\alpha})\\
&=-\frac{1}{z(1+\xi^{2}_{1}m_{1n}(\mu_{1}))}+\frac{z\xi_{1}^{2}(m_{1n}(z)-m_{1n}(\mu_{1}))}{(z(1+\xi^{2}_{1}m_{1n}(\mu_{1})))(z(1+\xi^{2}_{1}m_{1n}(z)))}+\mathrm{O}_{\prec}(n^{-1/2-1/\alpha})\\
&=\frac{1}{zd_{1}m_{1n}(\mu_{1})}-\frac{z\xi_{1}^{2}(m_{1n}(z)-m_{1n}(\mu_{1}))}{zd_{1}m_{1n}(\mu_{1})}\left(\mathcal{G}_{11}+\mathrm{O}_{\prec}(n^{-1/2-2/\alpha})\right)+\mathrm{O}_{\prec}(n^{-1/2-1/\alpha})\\
&=\frac{1}{zd_{1}m_{1n}(\mu_{1})}-\frac{z\xi_{1}^{2}}{zd_{1}m_{1n}(\mu_{1})}(\mathcal{G}_{11}+\mathrm{O}_{\prec}(n^{-1/2-1/\alpha}))\times\mathrm{O}_{\prec}(n^{-1/\alpha})+\mathrm{O}_{\prec}(n^{-1/2-1/\alpha}),\end{split}

(B.25)

where in the fourth step we used Lemma
A.1
. By Lemma
A.1
, (
C.7
) and (
A.21
), this yields that for some constant
C>0

\displaystyle\begin{split}|\mathcal{G}_{11}|&=\frac{1}{|zd_{1}m_{1n}(\mu_{1})|}+\mathrm{O}_{\prec}(n^{-1/2-1/\alpha})\\
&=\frac{C}{d_{1}}+\mathrm{O}_{\prec}(n^{-1/2-1/\alpha}).\end{split}

(B.26)

Similarly, when
2\leqslant i\leqslant n,
by (
A.21
) and the definition of
d_{1},
using Lemma
A.1
, we see that

\mathcal{G}_{ii}=\mathrm{O}_{\prec}(n^{-1/\alpha}).

(B.27)

We also provide some basic controls for the matrix
\mathcal{G}^{(i)}
for all
1\leqslant i\leqslant n.
By definition and an elementary calculation, it is not hard to see that

\mathcal{G}_{ii}^{(i)}=-z^{-1};\ \mathcal{G}_{ik}^{(i)}=0,\ 1\leqslant k\neq i\leqslant n.

(B.28)

Moreover, using (
B.24
), (
B.27
) and the third identity of Lemma
A.12
, we find that for
1\leqslant i\leqslant n,

\mathcal{G}_{kk}^{(i)}=\mathrm{O}_{\prec}(n^{-1/\alpha}),\ k\neq i;\ \mathcal{G}_{kl}^{(i)}=\mathrm{O}_{\prec}(n^{-1/2-1/\alpha}),\ k,l\neq i;

(B.29)

With the above preparation, we now proceed to the control of
Z_{i}
in (
B.9
), unlike in (
B.10
), since
Q
and
\mathcal{Q}
have the same non-zero eigenvalues, we control it as follows using the above bounds

\begin{split}Z_{i}&\prec\frac{\xi^{2}_{i}}{n}\|G^{(i)}\Sigma\|_{F}\prec\frac{\xi^{2}_{i}}{n}\|G^{(i)}\|_{F}=\frac{\xi^{2}_{i}}{n}(\operatorname{tr}((G^{(i)})^{2}))^{1/2}\leq\frac{\xi^{2}_{i}}{n}\operatorname{tr}((\mathcal{G}^{(i)})^{2})^{1/2}+\frac{\xi_{i}^{2}}{n}\frac{\sqrt{|n-p|}}{|z|}\\
&\asymp\frac{\xi^{2}_{i}}{n}\|\mathcal{G}^{(i)}\|_{F}+\frac{\xi^{2}_{i}}{n}\frac{n^{1/2}}{n^{1/\alpha}}\\
&=\frac{\xi^{2}_{i}}{n}\left((\mathcal{G}_{ii}^{(i)})^{2}+\sum_{j\neq i}(\mathcal{G}^{(i)}_{jj})^{2}+\sum_{j\neq k\neq i}(\mathcal{G}^{(i)}_{jk})^{2}\right)^{1/2}+\frac{\xi^{2}_{i}}{n}\frac{n^{1/2}}{n^{1/\alpha}}\\
&\prec\frac{\xi_{1}^{2}}{n}\left(|z|^{-2}+nn^{-2/\alpha}+n^{2}n^{-1-2/\alpha}\right)^{1/2}+\frac{\xi^{2}_{i}}{n}\frac{n^{1/2}}{n^{1/\alpha}}\\
&\prec\frac{\xi_{i}^{2}}{n^{1/2+1/\alpha}},\end{split}

(B.30)

where in the third and fourth steps we used (
B.28
) and (
B.29
).

Besides, unlike in (
B.11
), by a discussion similar to (
B.30
), we now have from Lemma
A.14
that

\begin{split}T_{i}:=\frac{1}{n}{\rm tr}G^{(i)}\Sigma-m_{1}(z)&=\frac{1}{n}\mathbf{y}_{i}^{*}G\Sigma G^{(i)}\mathbf{y}_{i}=\frac{1}{n}\frac{\mathbf{y}^{*}_{i}G^{(i)}\Sigma G^{(i)}\mathbf{y}_{i}}{1+\mathbf{y}^{*}_{i}G^{(i)}\mathbf{y}_{i}}\\
&\prec\frac{\xi^{2}_{i}}{n}\frac{n^{-1}\|G^{(i)}\|^{2}_{F}}{|1+\mathbf{y}^{*}_{i}G^{(i)}\mathbf{y}_{i}|}\\
&\prec\frac{\xi^{2}_{i}}{n^{2}}|z||\mathcal{G}_{ii}|\left(\|\mathcal{G}^{(i)}\|^{2}_{F}+\frac{n}{n^{2/\alpha}}\right),\\
&\prec\frac{\xi_{i}^{2}}{n^{1+2/\alpha}}.\end{split}

(B.31)

where in the second step we used the relation (
B.13
), in the third step we used Lemma
A.12
and in the last two steps we used a discussion similar to (
B.30
).

With the above control, we now use an idea similar to the proof of Lemma
B.3
to conclude the proof. The key ingredient is to explore the relation of
m_{1}
and
m_{2}.
We start with
m_{2}.
Using the above notations and Lemma
A.12
, we find that

\frac{1}{-z(1+\xi_{i}^{2}m_{1}(z))}=\frac{1}{\mathcal{G}_{ii}^{-1}+z(Z_{i}+T_{i})}.

(B.32)

Consequently, by (
B.26
), (
B.27
), (
B.30
) and (
B.31
), we see that

\frac{1}{-z(1+\xi_{i}^{2}m_{1}(z))}\prec n^{-1/\alpha}.

(B.33)

Then using the decomposition as in (
B.9
), we have that

\displaystyle\begin{split}m_{2}&=\frac{1}{n}\frac{\xi^{2}_{1}}{-z(1+\xi^{2}_{1}n^{-1}\operatorname{tr}G^{(1)}\Sigma+Z_{1})}+\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=2}^{n}\frac{\xi^{2}_{i}}{-z(1+\xi^{2}_{i}n^{-1}\operatorname{tr}G^{(i)}\Sigma+Z_{i})}\\
&=\frac{1}{n}\frac{\xi^{2}_{1}}{-z(1+\xi^{2}_{1}m_{1}(z)+\xi_{1}^{2}n^{-1-2/\alpha}+Z_{1})}+\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=2}^{n}\frac{\xi^{2}_{i}}{-z(1+\xi^{2}_{i}m_{1}(z)+\xi_{i}^{2}n^{-1-2/\alpha}+Z_{i})}\\
&=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\frac{\xi^{2}_{i}}{-z(1+\xi^{2}_{i}m_{1}(z))}+\mathrm{O}_{\prec}\left(n^{-3/2}+\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=2}^{n}\frac{\xi^{4}_{i}}{|z|n^{1/2+1/\alpha}}\right)\\
&=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\frac{\xi^{2}_{i}}{-z(1+\xi^{2}_{i}m_{1}(z))}+\mathrm{O}_{\prec}(n^{-1/2-1/\alpha}),\end{split}

(B.34)

where in the second step we used (
B.31
), in the third step we used a discussion similar to (
B.25
) and in the last step we used (
A.21
). Then we study
m_{1}
using the arguments as between (
B.14
) and (
B.21
). We provide the key ingredients as follows. First, for
\mathtt{R}_{11}
in (
B.15
), note that by definition of
m_{2}^{(i)}
and (
B.28
), we have that

\begin{split}\left|m^{(i)}_{2}\right|&\leq\frac{1}{n}\left(\sum_{j\neq i}\xi^{2}_{j}|\mathcal{G}^{(i)}_{jj}|+|z|^{-1}\right)\\
&\leq\frac{1}{n}\left[\sum_{j\neq 1}\xi^{2}_{j}\left(|\mathcal{G}_{jj}|+\frac{|\mathcal{G}_{j1}||\mathcal{G}_{1j}|}{|\mathcal{G}_{11}|}\right)+|z|^{-1}\right]\\
&\prec n^{-1/\alpha},\end{split}

where in the second step we used Lemma
A.12
and in the last step we used (
B.24
), (
B.27
) and (
A.21
). Moreover, by Lemma
A.12
and (
B.27
), we find that
(1+\mathbf{y}_{i}^{*}G^{(i)}\mathbf{y}_{i})^{-1}\prec 1.
Therefore, we conclude that for all
1\leqslant i\leqslant n,

\begin{split}&{\rm tr}\left(\frac{G^{(i)}(\mathbf{y}_{i}\mathbf{y}_{i}^{*}-n^{-1}\xi^{2}_{i}\Sigma)}{1+\mathbf{y}_{i}^{*}G^{(i)}\mathbf{y}_{i}}(I+m_{2}^{(i)}\Sigma)^{-1}\Sigma\right)\asymp{\rm tr}\left(\xi^{2}_{i}G^{(i)}(\mathbf{u}_{i}\mathbf{u}_{i}^{*}-n^{-1}I)(I+m_{2}^{(i)}\Sigma)^{-1}\Sigma^{2}\right)\\
&=\xi_{i}^{2}\left(\mathbf{u}_{i}^{*}G^{(i)}(I+m_{2}^{(i)}\Sigma)^{-1}\Sigma^{2}\mathbf{u}_{i}-n^{-1}{\rm tr}\left(G^{(i)}(I+m_{2}^{(i)}\Sigma)^{-1}\Sigma^{2}\right)\right)\\
&\prec\frac{\xi_{i}^{2}}{n}\|G^{(i)}\|_{F}\prec\frac{\xi_{i}^{2}}{n^{1/2+1/\alpha}},\end{split}

where in the last step we used a discussion similar to (
B.30
). Together with (
A.21
), we can conclude that
\mathtt{R}_{11}\prec n^{-1/2-1/\alpha}.
For
\mathtt{R}_{12},
using (
B.19
), (
B.24
) and (
B.27
)

m_{2}-m^{(i)}_{2}\prec\frac{1}{n}\sum_{j\neq i}\frac{\xi^{2}_{i}n^{-1-2/\alpha}}{n^{-1/\alpha}}+\frac{1}{n}\prec n^{-1}.

Then by an argument similar to (
B.20
), we can conclude that
\mathtt{R}_{12}\prec n^{-1-1/\alpha}.
Similarly, for
R_{2},
we have that

\begin{split}&\frac{1}{n}\left|{\rm tr}\left(\frac{(G^{(i)}-G)\Sigma(I+m_{2}\Sigma)^{-1}\Sigma}{1+\mathbf{y}_{i}^{*}G^{(i)}\mathbf{y}_{i}}\right)\right|=\frac{1}{n}\left|\frac{\mathbf{y}_{i}^{*}G^{(i)}\Sigma(I+m_{2}\Sigma)^{-1}\Sigma G\mathbf{y}_{i}}{1+\mathbf{y}_{i}^{*}G^{(i)}\mathbf{y}_{i}}\right|\\
&\asymp\frac{1}{n}\left|\mathbf{y}_{i}^{*}G^{(i)}\Sigma(I+m_{2}\Sigma)^{-1}\Sigma G^{(i)}\mathbf{y}_{i}\right|\prec\frac{\xi_{i}^{2}}{n^{2}}\|G^{(i)}\|_{F}\|G\|_{F}\prec\frac{\xi_{i}^{2}}{n^{1+2/\alpha}}.\end{split}

Consequently, we have that

\frac{z}{n}\left|{\rm tr}(R_{2}\Sigma)\right|\prec\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i}\frac{\xi_{i}^{2}}{n^{1+1/\alpha}}\prec n^{-1-1/\alpha}.

Combining all the above arguments, we find that (
B.21
) still holds true. Armed with all the above controls, using an argument similar to the discussions between (
B.22
) and (
B.23
), we can conclude the proof.

∎

Combining the above two lemmas, we now proceed to the proof of Proposition
B.1
. We will use a continuity argument as in
[
27
, Lemma A.12]
or
[
14
, Section 4.1]
. In fact, our discussion is easier since the real part in the spectral domain
\widetilde{\mathbf{D}}_{u}
is divergent so that the rate is independent of
\eta
. Due to similarity, we focus on explaining the key ingredients.

Proof of Proposition
B.1
.

As before, due to similarity, we focus on the study of the separable covariance i.i.d. model as in Case (2) of Assumption
2.1
when
\xi^{2}
satisfies (
2.5
).

For each
z=E+\mathrm{i}\eta\in\widetilde{\mathbf{D}}_{u}
, we fix the real part and construct a sequence
\{\eta_{j}\}
by setting
\eta_{j}=\mathtt{C}\mu_{1}-jn^{-3}
. Then it is clear that
\eta
falls in an interval
[\eta_{j-1},\eta_{j}]
for some
0\leq j\leq Cn^{1/\alpha+3},C>0
is some constant.

In Lemma
B.3
, we have proved that the results hold for
\eta_{0}
. Now we assume (
B.2
) holds for some
\eta_{j}
. Then according to Lemma
B.4
, we have that

|m_{1}(z_{j})-m_{1n}(z_{j})|+|m_{Q}(z_{j})-m_{n}(z_{j})|\prec n^{-1/2-2/\alpha},\ |m_{2}(z_{j})-m_{2n}(z_{j})|\prec n^{-1/2-1/\alpha}.

For any
\eta^{\prime}
lying in the interval
[\eta_{k-1},\eta_{k}]
, denote
z^{\prime}=E+\mathrm{i}\eta^{\prime}
and
z_{j}=E+\mathrm{i}\eta_{j}
. According to the first resolvent identity, we have that

\|\mathcal{G}(z^{\prime})-\mathcal{G}(z_{j})\|\leqslant n^{-3}\|\mathcal{G}(z^{\prime})\|\|\mathcal{G}(z_{j})\|\prec n^{-11/6-1/\alpha},

(B.35)

where in the second step we used the basic bound
\|\mathcal{G}(z^{\prime})\|\leqslant n^{2/3},
(
B.24
), (
B.27
) and Gershgorin circle theorem.

On the one hand, according to the definitions in (
A.20
), using the first resolvent identity, we have that

\begin{split}m_{1}(z^{\prime})-m_{1}(z_{j})&=\frac{1}{n}{\rm tr}[(G(z^{\prime})-G(z_{j}))\Sigma]=\frac{1}{n^{4}}{\rm tr}(G(z^{\prime})G(z_{j})\Sigma)\prec\frac{1}{n^{4}\eta_{j}}\|G(z_{j})\|_{F}\prec n^{-17/6-1/\alpha},\end{split}

where in the last step we used a discussion similar to (
B.30
). Similarly, combining (
B.35
) and (
A.21
), we have that

m_{2}(z^{\prime})-m_{2}(z_{j})=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\xi^{2}_{i}(\mathcal{G}_{ii}(z^{\prime})-\mathcal{G}_{ii}(z_{j}))\prec n^{-11/6-1/\alpha},

and by a discussion similar to (
B.30
)

\displaystyle|m_{Q}(z^{\prime})-m_{Q}(z_{j})|

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{p}\left|\operatorname{tr}\left(G(z^{\prime})-G(z_{j})\right)\right|\leqslant n^{-4}\|G(z^{\prime})\|_{F}\|G(z_{j})\|_{F}

\displaystyle\prec n^{-4}(n^{1-2/\alpha})^{1/2}n^{1/2+2/3}=n^{-7/3-1/\alpha}.

On the other hand, using the definitions in (
2.16
), we decompose that

\begin{split}m_{1n}(z^{\prime})-m_{1n}(z_{j})&=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i}\left(\frac{\sigma_{i}}{-z^{\prime}(1+\sigma_{i}m_{2n}(z^{\prime}))}-\frac{\sigma_{i}}{-z^{\prime}(1+\sigma_{i}m_{2n}(z_{j}))}\right)\\
&+\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i}\left(\frac{\sigma_{i}}{-z^{\prime}(1+\sigma_{i}m_{2n}(z_{j}))}-\frac{\sigma_{i}}{-z_{j}(1+\sigma_{i}m_{2n}(z_{j}))}\right)\\
&:=\mathcal{M}_{11}+\mathcal{M}_{12}.\end{split}

For
\mathcal{M}_{11}
, according to Lemma
A.1
and (
2.16
), we readily obtain that

\begin{split}\mathcal{M}_{11}&=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i}\frac{\sigma^{2}_{i}}{-z^{\prime}(1+\sigma_{i}m_{2n}(z^{\prime}))(1+\sigma_{i}m_{2n}(z_{j}))}\big(m_{2n}(z_{j})-m_{2n}(z^{\prime})\big)\\
&=\mathrm{O}(|z^{\prime}|^{-1})\times\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i}\Big(\frac{\xi^{2}_{i}}{-z_{j}(1+\xi^{2}_{i}m_{1n}(z_{j}))}-\frac{\xi^{2}_{i}}{-z_{j}(1+\xi^{2}_{i}m_{1n}(z^{\prime}))}\Big)\\
&+\mathrm{O}(|z^{\prime}|^{-1})\times\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i}\left(\frac{\xi^{2}_{i}}{-z_{j}(1+\xi^{2}_{i}m_{1n}(z^{\prime}))}-\frac{\xi^{2}_{i}}{-z^{\prime}(1+\xi^{2}_{i}m_{1n}(z^{\prime}))}\right)\\
&=\mathrm{O}(|z^{\prime}|^{-1})\times\left(\mathtt{M}_{11,1}+\mathtt{M}_{11,2}\right).\end{split}

For
\mathtt{M}_{11,1},
by a discussion similar to (
B.26
) and (
B.27
), we find that

\begin{split}\mathtt{M}_{11,1}&=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\frac{\xi_{i}^{4}}{-z_{j}(1+\xi^{2}_{i}m_{1n}(z^{\prime}))(1+\xi^{2}_{i}m_{1n}(z_{j}))}(m_{1n}(z^{\prime})-m_{1n}(z_{j}))\\
&\prec\left(\frac{\xi_{1}^{4}}{-nz_{j}d^{2}_{1}|m_{1n}(z_{j})||m_{1n}(z^{\prime})|}+\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i\geq 2}\frac{\xi_{i}^{4}}{|z^{\prime}(1+\xi^{2}_{1}m_{1n}(z^{\prime}))(1+\xi^{2}_{i}m_{1n}(z_{j}))|}\right)\times|m_{1n}(z_{j})-m_{1n}(z^{\prime})|\\
&\prec\mathrm{o}(1)\times|m_{1n}(z_{j})-m_{1n}(z^{\prime})|.\end{split}

Similarly, for
\mathtt{M}_{11,2},
we have that

\begin{split}\mathtt{M}_{11,2}=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i}\frac{\xi_{i}^{2}(z_{j}-z^{\prime})}{z^{\prime}z_{j}(1+\xi_{i}^{2}m_{1n}(z^{\prime}))}\prec\frac{z^{\prime}-z_{j}}{z^{\prime}z_{j}}\prec n^{-3-2/\alpha}.\end{split}

Analogously, we can prove that
\mathcal{M}_{12}\prec n^{-3-4/\alpha}
.
Therefore, combining the above bounds with (
A.21
), we see that

|m_{1n}(z^{\prime})-m_{1n}(z_{j})|\prec n^{-3-2/\alpha}.

By similar procedures and arguments, we can also prove that

|m_{2n}(z^{\prime})-m_{2n}(z_{j})|\prec n^{-3-2/\alpha},\ |m_{n}(z^{\prime})-m_{n}(z_{j})|\prec n^{-3-2/\alpha}.

and

\displaystyle\left\|(z^{\prime})^{-1}(I+m_{1n}(z^{\prime})D^{2})^{-1}-(z_{j})^{-1}(I+m_{1n}(z_{j})D^{2})^{-1}\right\|\prec n^{-3-2/\alpha}.

Therefore, combining all the above bounds with triangle inequality, we see that the results of part 1 of Theorem
A.7
hold for
z^{\prime}.
Using an induction procedure and a standard lattice argument (for example, see
[
14
,
27
]
), we find that the results hold for all
z\in\widetilde{\mathbf{D}}_{u}
and conclude the proof of Proposition
B.1
.

∎

B.1.2
Proof of Theorem
A.7

Once Proposition
B.1
is proved, we can roughly locate the edge eigenvalues of
Q
as in Lemma
B.2
so that we can expand the spectral domain from
\widetilde{\mathbf{D}}_{u}
to
\mathbf{D}_{u}
for
Q^{(1)}
and conclude the proof of Theorem
A.7
.

As before, we focus on the study of the separable covariance i.i.d. model as in Case (2) of Assumption
2.1
when
\xi^{2}
satisfies (
2.5
). Recall the definitions of
\mu_{2}
and
\lambda_{1}^{(1)}
around (
C.1
) and in Figure
7
. By Lemma
B.2
and an analogous argument, as well as
Weyl’s inequality, we find that conditional on the event
\Omega,
with high probability,

\mu_{1}>\lambda_{1}>\mu_{2}>\lambda_{1}^{(1)}.

(B.36)

By (
C.7
) and a similar argument, we see that
\mu_{k}\asymp\xi_{k}^{2},k=1,2.
Together with (
A.21
), we have that
\mu_{1}-\mu_{2}\geq C_{1}n^{1/\alpha}\log^{-1}n
for some constant
C_{1}>0
on the event
\Omega.
This implies for some constant
C>0,
for
z\in\mathbf{D}_{u},

\displaystyle|\lambda_{1}^{(1)}-z|\geq Cn^{1/\alpha}\log^{-1}n,

(B.37)

Now we proceed to the proof of Theorem
A.7
. Recall (
A.3
) and (
A.4
).

Proof of Theorem
A.7
.

Again, we focus on the study of the separable covariance i.i.d. model as in Case (2) of Assumption
2.1
when
\xi^{2}
satisfies (
2.5
).

Observe by (
B.37
) that it holds uniformly for
z\in\mathbf{D}_{u}
and
\mathcal{T}\subset\{2,\dots,n\}
, for some constant
C_{1}>0

\displaystyle\|G^{(1\mathcal{T})}\|\leqslant C_{1}n^{-1/\alpha}\log n.

(B.38)

By the definition of
m_{2}^{(1)}
and a decomposition similar to (
B.9
), we have that

\displaystyle m_{2}^{(1)}=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=2}^{n}\frac{\xi^{2}_{i}}{-z-z\mathbf{y}_{i}^{*}G^{(1i)}\mathbf{y}_{i}}=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=2}^{n}\frac{\xi^{2}_{i}}{-z(1+\xi^{2}_{i}n^{-1}\operatorname{tr}G^{(1i)}\Sigma+Z_{i}^{(1)})},

\displaystyle Z_{i}^{(1)}=\mathbf{y}_{i}^{*}G^{(1i)}\mathbf{y}_{i}-\xi^{2}_{i}n^{-1}\operatorname{tr}G^{(1i)}\Sigma.

By arguments similar to (
B.10
) and (
B.11
) but with (
B.38
), we obtain that

\displaystyle Z_{i}^{(1)}\prec\frac{\xi^{2}_{i}}{n}\|G^{(1i)}\Sigma\|_{F}\leq\frac{\xi^{2}_{i}}{n}\|G^{(1i)}\|\|\Sigma\|_{F}\prec\frac{\xi^{2}_{i}}{n^{1/2}}n^{-1/\alpha},

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n}\operatorname{tr}(G^{(1i)}\Sigma)-m_{1}^{(1)}(z)=\frac{1}{n}\mathbf{y}^{*}G^{(1)}\Sigma G^{(1i)}\mathbf{y}_{i}\prec\frac{\xi^{2}_{i}}{n}n^{-2/\alpha}.

In addition, using (
B.38
) and a discussion similar to (
B.32
)–(
B.34
), we readily see that

\displaystyle m^{(1)}_{2}=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=2}^{n}\frac{\xi^{2}_{i}}{-z(1+\xi^{2}_{i}m^{(1)}_{1})}+\mathrm{O}_{\prec}\left(n^{-1/2-1/\alpha}\right).

Using the decomposition

\displaystyle Q^{(1)}-zI=\sum_{i=2}^{n}\mathbf{y}_{i}\mathbf{y}_{i}^{*}+zm^{(1)}_{2}(z)\Sigma-z(I+m_{2}^{(1)}(z)\Sigma),

by arguments similar to (
B.14
)–(
B.21
) with
\|\mathcal{G}^{(1\mathcal{T})}\|=\|G^{(1\mathcal{T})}\|\prec n^{-1/\alpha}
, we conclude that

\displaystyle\begin{split}m_{1}^{(1)}&=-z^{-1}\frac{1}{n}\operatorname{tr}((I+m_{2}^{(1)}(z)\Sigma)^{-1}\Sigma)+\mathrm{O}_{\prec}\left(n^{-1/2-2/\alpha}\right)\\
&=-\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{p}\frac{\sigma_{i}}{z(1+m_{2}^{(1)}\sigma_{i})}+\mathrm{O}_{\prec}\left(n^{-1/2-2/\alpha}\right).\end{split}

Combining with the definitions in (
2.16
), we see that

\displaystyle\begin{split}m_{1}^{(1)}(z)-m_{1n}^{(1)}(z)&=-\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{p}\frac{\sigma_{i}}{z(1+\sigma_{i}m_{2}^{(1)}(z))}+\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{p}\frac{\sigma_{i}}{z(1+\sigma_{i}m_{2n}^{(1)}(z))}+\mathrm{O}_{\prec}(n^{-1/2-2/\alpha})\\
&=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{p}\frac{\sigma_{i}^{2}(m_{2}^{(1)}(z)-m_{2n}^{(1)}(z))}{z(1+\sigma_{i}m_{2n}^{(1)}(z))(1+\sigma_{i}m_{2}^{(1)}(z))}+\mathrm{O}_{\prec}(n^{-1/2-2/\alpha})\\
&=\left(\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{p}\frac{\sigma_{i}^{2}}{z(1+\sigma_{i}m_{2n}^{(1)}(z))(1+\sigma_{i}m_{2}^{(1)}(z))}\right)\left(\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=2}^{n}\frac{\xi^{4}_{i}(m_{1}^{(1)}(z)-m_{1n}^{(1)}(z))}{z(1+\xi^{2}_{i}m_{1}^{(1)}(z))(1+\xi^{2}_{i}m_{1n}^{(1)}(z))}\right)+\mathrm{O}_{\prec}(n^{-1/2-2/\alpha})\\
&=\mathrm{o}(1)(m_{1}^{(1)}(z)-m_{1n}^{(1)}(z))+\mathrm{O}_{\prec}(n^{-1/2-2/\alpha}),\end{split}

where in the third step we used a discussion similar to (
B.33
) and (
A.21
). This completes our proof.

∎

B.2
Bounded support setting: proof of Theorem
A.8

In this section, we will prove Theorem
A.8
. In Section
B.2.1
, we study
m_{1n,c}(z)-m_{1n}(z)
and
m_{n,c}-m_{n},
which is a counterpart of Lemma 4.4 of
[
60
]
. Then in Section
B.2.2
, we study
m_{1n}(z)-m_{1}(z)
and
m_{Q}-m_{n},
which is a counterpart of Proposition 5.1 of
[
60
]
. Due to similarity, we only provide the details for the separable covariance i.i.d data as in (2) of Assumption
2.1
. The elliptical data can be handled similarly.

B.2.1
Control of
m_{1n,c}(z)-m_{1n}(z)
and
m_{n,c}(z)-m_{n}(z)

Due to similarity, we focus on
|m_{1n,c}-m_{1n}|
and briefly discuss
|m_{n,c}-m_{n}|
in the end. The proof ideas follow Lemma 4.5 of
[
62
]
or Lemma 4.4 of
[
60
]
. We focus on explaining the parts deviates the most.

Proof.

According to the definitions of
m_{1n,c}
and
m_{1n}
in (
2.17
) and (
2.16
), we observe that

\displaystyle|m_{1n,c}(z)-m_{1n}(z)|

\displaystyle\leqslant\left|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{p}\frac{\sigma_{i}}{-z+\sigma_{i}\int\frac{s}{1+sm_{1n,c}(z)}\mathrm{d}F(s)}-\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{p}\frac{\sigma_{i}}{-z+\frac{\sigma_{i}}{n}\sum_{j=1}^{n}\frac{\xi^{2}_{j}}{1+\xi^{2}_{j}m_{1n,c}(z)}}\right|

\displaystyle+|m_{1n,c}(z)-m_{1n}(z)|\left|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{p}\frac{\frac{\sigma_{i}^{2}}{n}\sum_{j=1}^{n}\frac{\xi^{4}_{j}}{(1+\xi^{2}_{j}m_{1n}(z))(1+\xi^{2}_{j}m_{1n,c}(z))}}{(-z+\frac{\sigma_{i}}{n}\sum_{j=1}^{n}\frac{\xi^{2}_{j}}{1+\xi^{2}_{j}m_{1n}(z)})(-z+\frac{\sigma_{i}}{n}\sum_{j=1}^{n}\frac{\xi^{2}_{j}}{1+\xi^{2}_{j}m_{1n,c}(z)})}\right|

(B.39)

\displaystyle:=\mathsf{P}_{1}+\mathsf{P}_{2}.

On the one hand, for
\mathsf{P}_{1},
we have that

\mathsf{P}_{1}=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{p}\frac{\sigma_{i}^{2}|n^{-1}\sum_{j}\frac{\xi^{2}_{j}}{1+\xi^{2}_{j}m_{1n,c}(z)}-\int\frac{s}{1+sm_{1n,c}(z)}\mathrm{d}F(s)|}{|(-z+\frac{\sigma_{i}}{n}\sum_{j=1}^{n}\frac{\xi^{2}_{j}}{1+\xi^{2}_{j}m_{1n,c}(z)})(-z+\sigma_{i}\int\frac{s}{1+sm_{1n,c}(z)}\mathrm{d}F(s))|}.

Since
z\in\mathbf{D}_{b}^{\prime}\subset\mathbf{D}_{b},
according to Assumption
2.10
and the continuity of
m_{1n,c}
,
we conclude that
|-z+\sigma_{i}\int\frac{s}{1+sm_{1n,c}(z)}\mathrm{d}F(s))|\geqslant c
for some constant
c>0.
Together with (
A.23
), we show that
|-z+\frac{\sigma_{i}}{n}\sum_{j=1}^{n}\frac{\xi^{2}_{j}}{1+\xi^{2}_{j}m_{1n,c}(z)}|\geqslant c^{\prime}
for some
c^{\prime}>0
when
n
is sufficiently large. Using (
A.23
) again, we conclude that on
\Omega,
for some small constant
\epsilon>0
and some constant
C>0

\mathsf{P}_{1}\leqslant Cn^{-1/2+\epsilon}.

(B.40)

On the other hand, for
\mathsf{P}_{2},
for notional convenience, we further write it as
\mathsf{P}_{2}=|m_{1n,c}(z)-m_{1n}(z)|\times|\mathsf{T}|.
For
\mathsf{T},
by Cauchy-Schwarz inequality, we have that

|\mathsf{T}|\leqslant\mathsf{E}_{1}\mathsf{E}_{2},

(B.41)

where
\mathsf{E}_{k},k=1,2,
are defined as

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\mathsf{E}_{1}:=\left(\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{p}\frac{\frac{\sigma^{2}_{i}}{n}\sum_{j=1}^{n}\frac{\xi^{4}_{j}}{(1+\xi^{2}_{j}m_{1n}(z))^{2}}}{|-z+\frac{\sigma_{i}}{n}\sum_{j=1}^{n}\frac{\xi^{2}_{j}}{1+\xi^{2}_{j}m_{1n}(z)}|^{2}}\right)^{1/2},\\
&\mathsf{E}_{2}:=\left(\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{p}\frac{\frac{\sigma^{2}_{i}}{n}\sum_{j=1}^{n}\frac{\xi^{4}_{j}}{(1+\xi^{2}_{j}m_{1n,c}(z))^{2}}}{|-z+\frac{\sigma_{i}}{n}\sum_{j=1}^{n}\frac{\xi^{2}_{j}}{1+\xi^{2}_{j}m_{1n,c}(z)}|^{2}}\right)^{1/2}.\end{split}

(B.42)

Together with the identity (
D.21
) below and the fact
m_{1n}(z)\asymp 1
for
z\in\mathbf{D}_{b}^{\prime}
, we find that
\mathsf{E}_{1}\leqslant 1.
For the term
\mathsf{E}_{2},
we first consider a closely related quantity
\mathsf{W}(z)
defined as

\mathsf{W}(z):=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{p}\frac{\sigma_{i}^{2}\int\frac{s^{2}}{|1+sm_{1n,c}(z)|^{2}}\mathrm{d}F(s)}{|-z+\sigma_{i}\int\frac{s^{2}}{(1+sm_{1n,c}(z))^{2}}\mathrm{d}F(s)|^{2}}=1-\eta\frac{|m_{1n,c}(z)|^{2}}{\operatorname{Im}m_{1n,c}(z)}.

By assumption that
\phi^{-1}>\varsigma_{3},
(
3.7
) that
m_{1n,c}(L_{+})=-l^{-1}
and recall the notations in (
3.5
), we see that

\mathsf{W}(L_{+})<1.

(B.43)

Armed with (
B.43
), using (
A.23
) and Assumption
2.10
, we can apply an argument similar to Lemma A.6 of
[
62
]
or Lemma A.7 of
[
60
]
to conclude that when
n
is sufficiently large, for
z\in\mathbf{D}_{b},

\mathsf{E}^{2}_{2}=\mathsf{W}(L_{+})+\mathrm{o}(1)<\mathfrak{c}^{\prime},

(B.44)

for some constant
0<\mathfrak{c}^{\prime}<1.

Consequently, we find that when
n
is sufficiently large,
\mathsf{E}_{2}<1.
Together with (
B.41
), we can conclude that
|\mathsf{T}|<1
. This yields that

\mathsf{P}_{2}=\mathfrak{c}|m_{1n,c}-m_{1n}|,

for some constant
0<\mathfrak{c}<1.

Inserting the above control back into (
B.2.1
), using (
B.40
), we can conclude our proof that

m_{1n,c}=m_{1n}(z)+\mathrm{O}(n^{-1/2+\epsilon}).

(B.45)

The proof of
m_{n,c}-m_{n}
follows from an argument similar to (
B.2.1
) using (
2.16
) and (
2.17
) that

m_{n}(z)=\frac{1}{p}\sum_{i=1}^{p}\frac{1}{-z+\sigma_{i}n^{-1}\sum_{j=1}^{n}\frac{\xi_{j}^{2}}{1+\xi_{j}^{2}m_{1n}}},\ m_{n,c}(z)=\frac{1}{p}\sum_{i=1}^{p}\frac{1}{-z+\sigma_{i}\int_{0}^{l}\frac{s}{1+sm_{1n,c}(z)}\mathrm{d}F(s)},

the results of
m_{1n,c}-m_{1n}
and (
A.23
). We omit the details.
∎

B.2.2
Control of
m_{1n}(z)-m_{1}(z)
and
m_{n}(z)-m_{Q}(z)

Due to similarity, we focus on
|m_{1n,c}-m_{1n}|
and will briefly discuss how to study
|m_{Q}-m_{n}|
from line to line.
The proof ideas follow Proposition 5.1 of
[
62
]
or Proposition 5.1 of
[
60
]
. We focus on explaining the parts deviates the most. The proof relies on the following two lemmas.

Lemma B.5
.

Conditional on the event
\Omega
in Theorem
A.8
, for all
z=E+\mathrm{i}\eta\in\mathbf{D}_{b}^{\prime}
with
n^{-1/2+\epsilon_{d}}\leq\eta\leq n^{-1/(d+1)+\epsilon_{d}}
, we have

\displaystyle|m_{1n}(z)-m_{1}(z)|\prec\frac{1}{n\eta_{0}},\ |m_{n}(z)-m_{Q}(z)|\prec\frac{1}{n\eta_{0}}.

Lemma B.6
.

Assuming that
|m_{1n}(z)-m_{1}(z)|\prec n^{\epsilon_{d}}(n\eta_{0})^{-1}
, then conditional on the event
\Omega,
we have that for all
z\in\mathbf{D}_{b}^{\prime}

\displaystyle|m_{1n}(z)-m_{1}(z)|\prec\frac{1}{n\eta_{0}},\ |m_{n}(z)-m_{Q}(z)|\prec\frac{1}{n\eta_{0}}.

Armed with the above two lemmas, we now proceed to the control of
m_{1n}(z)-m_{1}(z).

Proof: control of
m_{1n}(z)-m_{1}(z)
and
m_{n}(z)-m_{Q}(z)
.

Due to similarity, we only prove
m_{1n}(z)-m_{1}(z).
We prove this by mathematical induction as that of Proposition 5.1 of
[
62
]
. Fix
E
such that
z=E+\mathrm{i}\eta_{0}\in\mathbf{D}_{b}^{\prime}
, we consider a sequence
(\eta_{j})
defined by
\eta_{j}=\eta_{0}+jn^{-2}
. Let
K
be the smallest positive integer such that
\eta_{K}\geq n^{-1/2+\epsilon_{d}}
. Note that for
j=K
, by Lemma
B.5
, we have that

\displaystyle|m_{1n}(z_{j})-m_{1}(z_{j})|\prec\frac{1}{n\eta_{0}}.

Then for any
z=E+\mathrm{i}\eta
with
\eta_{j-1}\leq\eta\leq\eta_{j}
, we have that for some constant
C>0

\displaystyle|m_{1}(z_{j})-m(z)|=\frac{1}{n}\operatorname{tr}\left[(G(z_{j})-G(z))\Sigma\right]=\frac{|z_{j}-z|}{n}\operatorname{tr}(G(z_{j})G(z)\Sigma)\leq C\frac{|z_{j}-z|}{\eta_{j-1}^{2}}\leq C\frac{n^{2\epsilon_{d}}}{n},

where we used the first resolvent identity and the trivial bound
|G(z)|\leqslant\eta^{-1}
, and similarly

\displaystyle|m_{1n}(z_{j})-m_{1n}(z)|=\left|\int\left[\frac{1}{x-z_{j}}-\frac{1}{x-z}\right]\rho(x)\mathrm{d}x\right|\leq\frac{|z_{j}-z|}{\eta_{j-1}^{2}}\leq\frac{n^{2\epsilon_{d}}}{n}.

Thus we find that if
|m_{1n}(z_{j})-m_{1}(z_{j})|\prec\frac{1}{n\eta_{0}}
, then by Lemma
B.6
, for some constant
C^{\prime}>0

\displaystyle|m_{1n}(z)-m_{1}(z)|\leq|m_{1n}(z_{j})-m_{1}(z_{j})|+\frac{C^{\prime}n^{2\epsilon_{d}}}{n}\prec\frac{n^{\epsilon_{d}}}{n\eta_{0}}.

(B.46)

This gives the result that
|m_{1n}(z)-m_{1}(z)|\prec(n\eta_{0})^{-1}
for
z=E+\mathrm{i}\eta
with
\eta_{j-1}\leq\eta\leq\eta_{j}
. The proof for each
z
can be completed by an induction on
j.
Finally, using an induction procedure and a standard lattice argument (for example, see
[
14
,
27
]
), we find that the results hold for all
z\in\mathbf{D}_{b}^{\prime}
. More specifically, we construct a lattice
\mathcal{L}
from
z^{\prime}=E^{\prime}+\mathrm{i}\eta_{0}\in\mathbf{D}_{b}^{\prime}
with
|z-z^{\prime}|\leq n^{-3}
. It is obvious that the bound holds uniformly on
\mathcal{L}
. For any
z=E+\mathrm{i}\eta_{0}\notin\mathcal{L}
, we find a
z^{\prime}\in\mathcal{L}
and then
|z-z^{\prime}|\leq n^{-3}
. Moreover, using resolvent identity, we can conclude that
|m_{1}(z)-m_{1}(z^{\prime})|\leq\eta^{-2}_{0}|z-z^{\prime}|\ll(n\eta_{0})^{-1}
. Therefore, we conclude the proof.

∎

In what follows, we prove lemmas
B.5
and
B.6
. The proofs are similar to those of Lemmas 5.6 and 5.7 of
[
62
]
, except that we will need a weak local law as follows.

Proposition B.7

(Weak averaged local law)
.

Suppose the assumptions of Theorem
A.8
hold. We have that for
z\in\mathbf{D}_{b}^{\prime}

|m_{Q}(z)-m_{n}(z)|+|m_{1}(z)-m_{1n}(z)|+|m_{2}(z)-m_{2n}(z)|=\mathrm{O}_{\prec}\left((n\eta)^{-1/4}\right).

Proof.

The proof of Proposition
B.7
is relatively standard in the random matrix literature, for example, see Section 4.1 of
[
14
]
or Section 3.6 of
[
37
]
or Appendix A.2 of
[
27
]
or Section 5.2 of
[
92
]
. Due to similarity, as in Lemma 5.12 of
[
92
]
, we only provide the key ingredients. Define the
z
-dependent parameter

\displaystyle\Psi(z):=\sqrt{\frac{\operatorname{Im}m_{1}(z)}{n\eta}}+\frac{1}{n\eta}.

(B.47)

Recall (
B.9
). By Lemma
A.14
and (
A.24
), we find that

\displaystyle Z_{i}\prec\frac{\xi^{2}_{i}}{n}\|G^{(i)}\Sigma^{1/2}\|_{F}\leqslant l\sqrt{\frac{\operatorname{Im}m^{(i)}_{1}(z)}{n\eta}}\asymp\Psi,

(B.48)

where in the last step we used (
A.25
). Together with (
A.25
) and the first equation of (
B.9
), we conclude that

\displaystyle m_{2}=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\frac{\xi^{2}_{i}}{-z(1+\xi^{2}_{i}m_{1}(z)+\mathrm{O}_{\prec}(\Psi))}.

(B.49)

For
m_{1}(z),
recall (
B.14
). According to the definition of
m_{1}(z)
in (
A.20
), we have that

\displaystyle m_{1}(z)=\frac{1}{n}\operatorname{tr}(G(z)\Sigma)=-\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{p}\frac{\sigma_{i}}{z(1+m_{2}(z)\sigma_{i})}+\frac{1}{n}\operatorname{tr}(R_{1}\Sigma)+\frac{1}{n}\operatorname{tr}(R_{2}\Sigma).

(B.50)

Similarly, for
m_{Q}(z)
in (
2.14
), we have that

m_{Q}(z)=\frac{1}{p}\operatorname{tr}(G(z))=-\frac{1}{p}\sum_{i=1}^{p}\frac{1}{z(1+m_{2}(z)\sigma_{i})}+\frac{1}{p}\operatorname{tr}(R_{1})+\frac{1}{p}\operatorname{tr}(R_{2}).

(B.51)

On the one hand, when
\eta\asymp 1,
by a discussion similar to (5.45) of
[
92
]
, we find that
\|(I+m_{2}^{(i)}\Sigma)^{-1}\|<\infty.
Then using (
B.48
), by a discussion similar to the equations between (
B.15
) and (
B.21
), we find that

\displaystyle m_{1}(z)=-\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{p}\frac{\sigma_{i}}{z(1+m_{2}(z)\sigma_{i})}+\mathrm{O}_{\prec}\Big(\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i}\frac{\xi^{2}_{i}\Psi}{z(1+\xi^{2}_{i}m_{1}(z)+\mathrm{O}_{\prec}(\Psi))}\Big).

(B.52)

Similarly, we have

m_{Q}(z)=-\frac{1}{p}\sum_{i=1}^{p}\frac{1}{z(1+m_{2}(z)\sigma_{i})}+\mathrm{O}_{\prec}\Big(\frac{1}{p}\sum_{i}\frac{\xi^{2}_{i}\Psi}{z(1+\xi^{2}_{i}m_{1}(z)+\mathrm{O}_{\prec}(\Psi))}\Big).

On the other hand, denote
\Xi:=\{|\mathcal{G}_{ij}(z)+\delta_{ij}(z(1+m_{1n}(z)\xi_{i}^{2}))^{-1}|+|m_{2}(z)-m_{2n}(z)|\leqslant(\log n)^{-1}\}.
When restricted on
\Xi,
by Assumption
2.10
, we also have that
\|(I+m_{2}^{(i)}\Sigma)^{-1}\|<\infty.
By an analogous argument, we find that (
B.52
) also holds true. By an argument similar to (
B.29
) using Lemma
A.12
, we have that for
i\neq j,

\mathbf{1}(\eta\geqslant 1)\mathcal{G}_{ij}\prec\Psi,\ \mathbf{1}(\Xi)\mathcal{G}_{ij}\prec\Psi.

The above arguments show that the counterparts of Lemmas 5.9 and 5.10 of
[
92
]
hold. Therefore, by the same arguments as in Lemma 5.12 of
[
92
]
, we can conclude the proof.

∎

Next we provide some useful controls whose proofs and results will be used in the proof of Lemmas
B.5
and
B.6
. The results and arguments are analogous to Lemma 5.4 of
[
62
]
. We only point out the key ingredients in our proof and refer the readers to
[
62
]
for more details.

Lemma B.8
.

Suppose the assumptions of Theorem
A.8
hold. Then we have that on
\Omega
and for all
z=E+\mathrm{i}\eta_{0}\in\mathbf{D}_{b}^{\prime}

\displaystyle\operatorname{Im}m_{1}(z)\prec\frac{1}{n\eta_{0}},\ \ \operatorname{Im}m_{Q}(z)\prec\frac{1}{n\eta_{0}}.

Proof.

Due to similarity, we focus our proof on
\operatorname{Im}m_{Q}(z).
We prove by contradiction. Given some
\epsilon>0,
conditional on
\Omega,
for some sufficiently small constants
0<c_{1},c_{2}<1,
we first introduce a probability event
\Xi_{1}\equiv\Xi_{1}(\epsilon)
so that the followings holds:

1.

For
z\in\mathbf{D}_{b}^{\prime},

|m_{Q}(z)-m_{n}(z)|+|m_{1}(z)-m_{1n}(z)|+|m_{2}(z)-m_{2n}(z)|\leqslant(n\eta)^{-1/4+c_{1}\epsilon}.

2.

For
z\in\mathbf{D}_{b}^{\prime}
and
Z_{i}
in (
B.9
),

\max_{i}Z_{i}\leqslant n^{c_{2}\epsilon}\Psi.

According to Proposition
B.7
and (
B.48
), we find that there exists some large constant
D>0
so that
\mathbb{P}(\Xi_{1})=1-n^{-D}.
In what follows, we restrict ourselves on
\Xi_{1}
so that the discussions are purely deterministic.

Assuming that

\operatorname{Im}m_{1}(z)>n^{\epsilon}\frac{1}{n\eta_{0}}.

We then conclude from the definition of
\Psi
in (
B.47
) that

\Psi=\mathrm{o}(\operatorname{Im}m_{1}(z)).

(B.53)

Moreover, by (
A.15
) and (
A.16
), we readily see that
\operatorname{Im}m_{1n}(z)\ll\operatorname{Im}m_{1}(z)
. This implies that for some constant
C>0

\displaystyle|m_{1n}(z)-m_{1}(z)|\geq|\operatorname{Im}m_{1n}-\operatorname{Im}m_{1}|>Cn^{\epsilon}\frac{1}{n\eta_{0}}.

(B.54)

On the other hand, by Proposition
B.7
and Assumption
2.10
, we see that (
B.52
) still holds. Together with
m_{1n}
in (
2.16
), using (
B.9
), we find that

\displaystyle m_{1n}-m_{1}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{p}\frac{\frac{\sigma_{i}^{2}}{n}\sum_{j}\frac{\xi^{4}_{j}(m_{1n}-m_{1})+\xi^{2}_{j}\mathrm{O}(n^{c_{2}\epsilon}\Psi)}{(1+\xi^{2}_{j}m_{1n})(1+\xi^{2}_{j}m_{1}+\mathrm{O}(n^{c_{2}\epsilon}\Psi))}}{(z-\frac{\sigma_{i}}{n}\sum_{j}\frac{\xi^{2}_{j}}{1+\xi^{2}_{j}m_{1n}})(z-\frac{\sigma_{i}}{n}\sum_{j}\frac{\xi^{2}_{j}}{1+\xi^{2}_{j}m_{1}+\mathrm{O}(n^{c_{2}\epsilon}\Psi)})}+\mathrm{O}(n^{c_{2}\epsilon}\Psi)

\displaystyle=\mathsf{C}_{1}(m_{1n}-m_{1})+\mathsf{C}_{2}+\mathrm{O}(n^{c_{2}\epsilon}\Psi),

(B.55)

where
\mathsf{C}_{1},\mathsf{C}_{2}
are defined as

\displaystyle\mathsf{C}_{1}:=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{p}\frac{\frac{\sigma_{i}^{2}}{n}\sum_{j}\frac{\xi^{4}_{j}}{(1+\xi^{2}_{j}m_{1n})(1+\xi^{2}_{j}m_{1}+\mathrm{O}(n^{c_{2}\epsilon}\Psi))}}{(z-\frac{\sigma_{i}}{n}\sum_{j}\frac{\xi^{2}_{j}}{1+\xi^{2}_{j}m_{1n}})(z-\frac{\sigma_{i}}{n}\sum_{j}\frac{\xi^{2}_{j}}{1+\xi^{2}_{j}m_{1}+\mathrm{O}(n^{c_{2}\epsilon}\Psi)})},

\displaystyle\mathsf{C}_{2}:=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{p}\frac{\frac{\sigma_{i}^{2}}{n}\sum_{j}\frac{\xi^{2}_{j}\mathrm{O}(n^{c_{2}\epsilon}\Psi)}{(1+\xi^{2}_{j}m_{1n})(1+\xi^{2}_{j}m_{1}+\mathrm{O}(n^{c_{2}\epsilon}\Psi))}}{(z-\frac{\sigma_{i}}{n}\sum_{j}\frac{\xi^{2}_{j}}{1+\xi^{2}_{j}m_{1n}})(z-\frac{\sigma_{i}}{n}\sum_{j}\frac{\xi^{2}_{j}}{1+\xi^{2}_{j}m_{1}+\mathrm{O}(n^{c_{2}\epsilon}\Psi)})}.

We first control
\mathsf{C}_{2}.
It is easy to see that
m_{1}\sim 1
by contradiction. If
m_{1}\ll 1
, one can observe from (
B.49
) that
m_{2}\sim 1
which yields
m_{1}\asymp 1
by (
B.52
). If
m_{1}\gg 1
, we have
m_{2}\ll 1
from (
B.49
), and then it gives that
m_{1}\asymp 1
by (
B.52
). Similarly, we can show that
m_{2}\asymp 1.
Together with Proposition
B.7
, we find that
m_{1n},m_{2n}\asymp 1.
Since
z\asymp 1,
using the definition of
m_{2n}
in (
2.16
) and Proposition
B.7
, we find that

\frac{1}{n}\sum_{j}\frac{\xi^{2}_{j}\mathrm{O}(n^{c_{2}\epsilon}\Psi)}{(1+\xi^{2}_{j}m_{1n})(1+\xi^{2}_{j}m_{1}+\mathrm{O}(n^{c_{2}\epsilon}\Psi))}=\mathrm{O}(n^{c_{2}\epsilon}\Psi).

Moreover, by Proposition
B.7
, (
B.45
) and Assumption
2.10
, we find that

\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i}\frac{1}{(z-\frac{\sigma_{i}}{n}\sum_{j}\frac{\xi^{2}_{j}}{1+\xi^{2}_{j}m_{1n}})(z-\frac{\sigma_{i}}{n}\sum_{j}\frac{\xi^{2}_{j}}{1+\xi^{2}_{j}m_{1}+\mathrm{O}(n^{c_{2}\epsilon}\Psi)})}\asymp 1.

This yields that

\mathsf{C}_{2}=\mathrm{O}(n^{c_{2}\epsilon}\Psi).

(B.56)

For
\mathsf{C}_{1},
using Proposition
B.7
and Assumption
2.10
, by an argument similar to (
B.41
), we can conclude that when
n
is sufficiently large, for some constant
0<\mathfrak{c}<1,

\mathsf{C}_{1}\leqslant\mathfrak{c}.

(B.57)

Combining (
B.55
), (
B.56
) and (
B.57
), we conclude that

\displaystyle|m_{1n}-m_{1}|=\mathrm{O}(n^{c_{2}\epsilon}\Psi),

which contradicts (
B.54
) since
c_{2}<1
is sufficiently small. This completes our proof for each fixed
z.
For uniformity in
z,
we can follow a standard lattice argument as discussed below (
B.46
). This finishes the proof. The discussion for
m_{Q}
follows from an analogous discussion with the help of (
B.49
) and (
B.52
).
∎

Remark B.9
.

Two remarks are in order. First, it is easy to see that repeating the proof of Lemma
B.8
, we can prove the results for all
\eta
as specified in (
A.2
). Second, we remark that combining (
B.48
) and Lemma
B.8
, when
z=E+\mathrm{i}\eta_{0}\in\mathbf{D}_{b}^{\prime},
we have that conditional on
\Omega

Z_{i}\prec\frac{1}{n\eta_{0}}.

Armed with the above discussions and results, following the strategies of Lemmas 5.6 and 5.7 of
[
62
]
or
[
60
]
, we prove Lemmas
B.5
and
B.6
using similar arguments as in Lemma
B.8
. Due to similarity, we only provide the key ingredients.

Proof of Lemmas
B.5
and
B.6
.

Due to similarity, we focus our proof on Lemma
B.5
and briefly mention that of Lemma
B.6
in the end. Due to similarity, we only explain
|m_{1n}(z)-m_{1}(z)|.

The proof is similar to that of Lemma
B.8
and we prove by contradiction. We also restrict ourselves on the event
\Xi_{1}
in Lemma
B.8
. We assume that
|m_{1n}(z)-m_{1}(z)|>n^{\epsilon}(n\eta_{0})^{-1}
. To see a contraction, in addition to the the arguments of Lemma
B.8
, we need to provide a finer control for
\Psi
since in the current proof it depends on
\eta
instead of
\eta_{0}.
Note that

\displaystyle\begin{split}n^{c_{2}\epsilon}\Psi&=n^{c_{2}\epsilon}\sqrt{\frac{|\operatorname{Im}m_{1}-\operatorname{Im}m_{1n}+\operatorname{Im}m_{1n}|}{n\eta}}+n^{c_{2}\epsilon}\frac{1}{n\eta}\\
&\leq n^{c_{2}\epsilon}\sqrt{\frac{|\operatorname{Im}m_{1}-\operatorname{Im}m_{1n}|}{n\eta}}+n^{c_{2}\epsilon}\sqrt{\frac{\operatorname{Im}m_{1n}}{n\eta}}+n^{c_{2}\epsilon}\frac{1}{n\eta}\\
&=\mathrm{o}(|m_{1}-m_{1n}|),\end{split}

(B.58)

where in the last step we used (
A.14
) and the assumption
|m_{1n}(z)-m_{1}(z)|>n^{\epsilon}(n\eta_{0})^{-1}\gg(n\eta)^{-1}
when
n^{-1/2+\epsilon_{d}}\leqslant\eta\leqslant n^{-1/(d+1)+\epsilon_{d}}.
Replacing
n^{c_{2}\epsilon}\Psi
with
\mathrm{o}(|m_{1}-m_{1n}|)
in the arguments between (
B.55
) and (
B.57
), we find that
|m_{1n}-m_{1}|=\mathrm{o}(|m_{1n}-m_{1}|)
which is a contraction. This proves the result for each fixed
z.
The uniformity follows from the same lattice argument as mentioned in the end of the proof of Lemma
B.8
.

The proof of Lemma
B.6
is similar. We also prove by contradiction and assume that
n^{\epsilon}(n\eta_{0})^{-1}<|m_{1}(z)-m_{1n}(z)|\leq n^{\epsilon+\epsilon_{d}}(n\eta_{0})^{-1}.
Under this assumption, together with (
A.15
) and (
A.16
), we find that (
B.58
) still holds true. The rest of the arguments are similar and we omit the details.

∎

Appendix C
Locations for extreme eigenvalues and proof of the main results

In this section, we study the first order convergent limits of the largest eigenvalues of
Q,
i.e.,
\lambda_{1}(Q).
In Section
C.1
, we investigate the case when
\{\xi_{i}^{2}\}
have unbounded support as in (i) of Assumption
2.4
. In Section
C.2
, we study the bounded support case as in (ii) of Assumption
2.4
.

C.1
The unbounded support case

In order to quantify the location of
\lambda_{1}\equiv\lambda_{1}(Q),
we need to introduce several auxiliary quantities. Recall
\mu_{1}
defined in (
3.14
). Similarly, we denote
\mu_{2}
by replacing
\xi^{2}_{(1)}
with
\xi^{2}_{(2)}
in (
3.14
). Moreover, for
d_{1}
and the sufficiently small constant
\epsilon>0
in (
3.15
), we denote

\mu_{1}^{\pm}:=\mu_{1}\pm n^{-1/2+2\epsilon}d_{1},

(C.1)

and recall that

Q^{(1)}:=Q-\mathbf{y}_{(1)}\mathbf{y}^{*}_{(1)},

(C.2)

where
\mathbf{y}_{(1)}
is the column of
Y
in (
2.1
) associated with
\xi^{2}_{(1)}
. Accordingly, we denote the largest eigenvalue of
Q^{(1)}
as
\lambda_{1}^{(1)}\equiv\lambda_{1}(Q^{(1)}).
Throughout this section, we shall prove Figure
7
so that the location of
\lambda_{1}
can be quantified with high probability on the event
\Omega
.

\lambda_{2}
\lambda_{1}^{(1)}
\mu_{2}
\mu_{1}^{-}
\mu_{1}
\mu_{1}^{+}
\lambda_{1}
is here

Figure 7:
Location of the largest eigenvalue of
Q
.

More formally, the main result is summarized in Proposition
C.1
below.

Proposition C.1
.

Suppose Assumptions
2.1
,
2.6
and (i) of Assumption
2.4
hold. For some sufficiently small constant
\epsilon>0
and
\mu_{1}^{\pm}
defined in (
C.1
), condition on the probability event
\Omega
in Lemma
A.11
, with high probability, we have that

\lambda_{1}\in[\mu_{1}^{-},\mu_{1}^{+}].

We now proceed to the proof of Proposition
C.1
following the structure described in Figure
7
.

Proof of Proposition
C.1
.

Due to similarity, we focus on the study of the separable covariance i.i.d. model as in Case (2) of Assumption
2.1
when
\xi^{2}
satisfies (
2.5
). The main differences from the other cases will be explained in the end of the proof.

In what follows, we restrict the discussion on the probability event
\Omega
in Lemma
A.11
.
By Weyl’s inequality, we have that
\lambda_{2}\leqslant\lambda_{1}^{(1)}.
Moreover, by (
B.36
), we see that with high probability
\lambda_{1}^{(1)}<\mu_{2}.
The rest of the proof leaves to prove that the following two claims:

\mu_{1}-\mu_{2}\geqslant n^{1/\alpha}\log^{-1}n,

(C.3)

and for
Q^{(1)}
in (
C.2
) and

M(\lambda)=1+\mathbf{y}^{*}_{(1)}G_{1}^{(1)}(\lambda)\mathbf{y}_{(1)},\ G^{(1)}_{1}(\lambda):=(Q^{(1)}-\lambda I)^{-1},

(C.4)

M(\lambda)
changes sign with high probability at
\mu_{1}^{-}
and
\mu_{1}^{+}.
In fact, for
\lambda_{1},
it should satisfy the following equation with high probability

{\rm det}(\lambda_{1}I-\mathbf{y}_{(1)}\mathbf{y}^{*}_{(1)}-Q^{(1)})=0\Rightarrow M(\lambda_{1})=0,

(C.5)

as long as
\lambda_{1}>\lambda_{1}^{(1)}.
On the other hand, if
M(\lambda)
changes sign at
\mu_{1}^{\pm},
by continuity, there must at least be an eigenvalue of
Q
in the interval
[\mu_{1}^{-},\mu_{1}^{+}].
If (
C.3
) holds, combining the above arguments, we see that the only possibility is
\lambda_{1}
and it is also true that
\lambda_{1}>\lambda_{1}^{(1)}.

We first justify (
C.3
). Recall that
\mu_{1}
is defined in (
3.14
) according to

1+(\xi^{2}_{(1)}+d_{1})m_{1n}(\mu_{1})=0.

Together with (
2.19
) and (
2.21
), we readily obtain that

1=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{p}\frac{\sigma_{i}}{\frac{\mu_{1}}{\xi^{2}_{(1)}+d_{1}}-\frac{\sigma_{i}}{n}\sum_{j=1}^{n}\frac{\xi_{(j)}^{2}}{\xi^{2}_{(1)}+d_{1}-\xi^{2}_{(j)}}}.

Recall (
A.5
). Using the definition of
d_{1}
and (
A.21
), we see that on
\Omega,
for some constant
C>0

\begin{split}\frac{1}{n}\sum_{j=1}^{n}\frac{\xi_{(j)}^{2}}{\xi^{2}_{(1)}+d_{1}-\xi^{2}_{(j)}}&=\frac{1}{n}\frac{\xi_{(1)}^{2}}{d_{1}}+\frac{1}{n}\sum_{j=2}^{n}\frac{\xi_{(j)}^{2}}{\xi^{2}_{(1)}+d_{1}-\xi^{2}_{(j)}}\\
&\leq\frac{Cn^{\epsilon}\log n}{n}+\frac{1}{n}\sum_{j=2}^{n}\frac{\xi_{(j)}^{2}}{\xi^{2}_{(1)}+d_{1}-\xi^{2}_{(2)}}\\
&\leq\frac{Cn^{\epsilon}\log n}{n}+\frac{C\log n}{n^{1/\alpha}}=Ce.\end{split}

(C.6)

Using the definition of
\varphi
in (
3.2
),
the above arguments imply that on
\Omega

\frac{\mu_{1}}{\xi_{(1)}^{2}+d_{1}}=\varphi+\mathrm{O}(e).

(C.7)

By an analogous argument, we have that for some constants
C_{k}>0,k=1,2,3,

\begin{split}&\frac{1}{n}\left(\sum_{j=1}^{n}\frac{\xi^{2}_{(j)}}{\xi^{2}_{(1)}+d_{1}-\xi^{2}_{(j)}}-\sum_{j=2}^{n}\frac{\xi^{2}_{(j)}}{\xi^{2}_{(2)}+d_{1}-\xi^{2}_{(j)}}\right)\\
&=\frac{1}{n}\left(\frac{\xi^{2}_{(1)}}{d_{1}}+\sum_{j=2}^{n}\frac{\xi^{2}_{(j)}}{\xi^{2}_{(1)}+d_{1}-\xi^{2}_{(j)}}-\sum_{j=2}^{n}\frac{\xi^{2}_{(j)}}{\xi^{2}_{(2)}+d_{1}-\xi^{2}_{(j)}}\right)\\
&=C_{1}e-\frac{1}{n}\sum_{j=2}^{n}\frac{\xi^{2}_{(j)}(\xi^{2}_{(1)}-\xi^{2}_{(2)})}{(\xi^{2}_{(1)}+d_{1}-\xi^{2}_{(j)})(\xi^{2}_{(2)}+d_{1}-\xi^{2}_{(j)})}\\
&\geq C_{1}e-\frac{1}{n}\sum_{j=2}^{n}\frac{\xi^{2}_{(j)}}{\xi^{2}_{(2)}+d_{1}-\xi^{2}_{(j)}}\\
&\geqslant C_{2}e,\end{split}

and on the other hand

\begin{split}&\frac{1}{n}\left(\sum_{j=1}^{n}\frac{\xi^{2}_{(j)}}{\xi^{2}_{(1)}+d_{1}-\xi^{2}_{(j)}}-\sum_{j=2}^{n}\frac{\xi^{2}_{(j)}}{\xi^{2}_{(2)}+d_{1}-\xi^{2}_{(j)}}\right)\\
&\leq\frac{1}{n}\left(\frac{\xi^{2}_{(1)}}{d_{1}}+\sum_{j=2}\frac{\xi^{2}_{(j)}}{\xi^{2}_{(1)}+d_{1}-\xi^{2}_{(j)}}+\sum_{j=2}\frac{\xi^{2}_{(j)}}{\xi^{2}_{(2)}+d_{1}-\xi^{2}_{(j)}}\right)\\
&\leqslant C_{3}e.\end{split}

Using the above control, the definition of
\mu_{2}
and a discussion similar to (
C.7
), we can prove that

\frac{\mu_{2}}{\xi_{(2)}^{2}+d_{1}}=\phi\bar{\sigma}+\mathrm{O}(e).

(C.8)

Combining (
C.7
) and (
C.8
), we immediately see that

\frac{\mu_{1}}{\xi^{2}_{(1)}+d_{1}}-\frac{\mu_{2}}{\xi^{2}_{(2)}+d_{1}}=\mathrm{O}(e).

(C.9)

This implies that

\begin{split}\mu_{1}-\mu_{2}&=(\xi^{2}_{(1)}+d_{1})\left(\frac{\mu_{1}}{\xi^{2}_{(1)}+d_{1}}-\frac{\mu_{2}}{\xi^{2}_{(2)}+d_{1}}\right)+\mu_{2}\left(\frac{\xi^{2}_{(1)}+d_{1}}{\xi^{2}_{(2)}+d_{1}}-1\right)\\
&=(\xi^{2}_{(1)}+d_{1})\left(\frac{\mu_{1}}{\xi^{2}_{(1)}+d_{1}}-\frac{\mu_{2}}{\xi^{2}_{(2)}+d_{1}}\right)+\frac{\mu_{2}}{\xi^{2}_{(2)}+d_{1}}\left(\xi^{2}_{(1)}-\xi^{2}_{(2)}\right)\\
&\geq n^{2/\alpha}\log^{-1}n,\end{split}

where in the third step we used (
C.9
), (
A.21
) and the definition of
e_{2}
in (
A.5
). This completes the proof of (
C.3
).

Next, we will show that

M(\mu_{1}^{-})<0,\ M(\mu_{1}^{+})>0.

Due to similarity, in what follows, we focus on the first inequality. Note that

\mathbf{y}_{1}^{*}G_{1}^{(1)}(\mu_{1}^{-})\mathbf{y}_{1}=\xi_{(1)}^{2}\mathbf{u}_{1}^{*}\Sigma^{1/2}G_{1}^{(1)}(\mu_{1}^{-})\Sigma^{1/2}\mathbf{u}_{1}.

Moreover, recall that
m_{1}^{(1)}(\mu_{1}^{-})=n^{-1}\operatorname{tr}(\Sigma^{1/2}G_{1}^{(1)}(\mu_{1}^{-})\Sigma^{1/2}).
Then according to Lemma
A.14
, we have that

\displaystyle\mathbf{y}_{1}^{*}G_{1}^{(1)}(\mu_{1}^{-})\mathbf{y}_{1}

\displaystyle=\xi^{2}_{(1)}m_{1}^{(1)}(\mu_{1}^{-})+\mathrm{O}_{\prec}\left(\frac{\xi^{2}_{(1)}}{n}\|G_{1}^{(1)}(\mu_{1}^{-})\|_{F}\right)

\displaystyle=\xi^{2}_{(1)}m_{1}^{(1)}(\mu_{1}^{-})+\mathrm{O}_{\prec}\left(\frac{\xi^{2}_{(1)}}{n^{1/2+1/\alpha}}\right),

(C.10)

where in the second step we used (
C.3
) and the fact
\mu_{2}>\lambda_{1}^{(1)}
. Moreover, for some sufficiently small constant
\epsilon_{0}>0
and
z_{0}=\mu_{1}^{-}+\mathrm{i}n^{-1/2-\epsilon_{0}},
we can decompose that

\displaystyle m_{1}^{(1)}(\mu_{1}^{-})

\displaystyle=\left[m_{1}^{(1)}(\mu_{1}^{-})-m_{1}^{(1)}(z_{0})\right]+\left[m_{1}^{(1)}(z_{0})-m_{1n}^{(1)}(z_{0})\right]+\left[m_{1n}^{(1)}(z_{0})-m_{1n}^{(1)}(\mu_{1}^{-})\right]+m_{1n}^{(1)}(\mu_{1}^{-})

\displaystyle=\mathsf{P}_{1}+\mathsf{P}_{2}+\mathsf{P}_{3}+m_{1n}^{(1)}(\mu_{1}^{-}).

(C.11)

First, by Theorem
A.7
, we have that
\mathsf{P}_{2}\prec n^{-1/2-2/\alpha}
. Second, let
\{\mathbf{v}^{(1)}_{i}\}
be the eigenvectors of
Q^{(1)}
associated with the eigenvalues
\{\lambda_{i}^{(1)}\},
then we have that

\begin{split}|\mathsf{P}_{1}|&\leq\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{p}|T\mathbf{v}^{(1)}_{i}|^{2}\left|\frac{1}{\lambda^{(1)}_{i}-\mu_{1}^{-}}-\frac{1}{\lambda^{(1)}_{i}-z_{0}}\right|\\
&=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{p}|T\mathbf{v}^{(1)}_{i}|^{2}\left|\frac{\mathrm{i}n^{-1/2-\epsilon_{0}}}{(\lambda^{(1)}_{i}-\mu_{1}^{-})(\lambda^{(1)}_{i}-z_{0})}\right|\\
&\leq\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{p}|T\mathbf{v}^{(1)}_{i}|^{2}\Big|\frac{\mathrm{i}n^{-1/2-\epsilon_{0}}+\mathrm{O}_{\prec}(n^{-2/\alpha-1/2}\log^{2}n)}{|\lambda^{(1)}_{i}-z_{0}|^{2}}\Big|\\
